Residual calculus

Theorem 31

(Residual calculus)
.

Assume that for each layer
n_{\mathrm{layer}}
there exist constants

d_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}\geq 0,\qquad\lambda_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}\geq 0,

and a scalar lower-triangular kernel

K_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}:\{(t,\tau):0\leq\tau<t\leq T-1\}\to[0,\infty)

such that for every
u\in\mathcal{X}_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}-1}
and every
0\leq\tau\leq t\leq T-1
,

\|J^{(n_{\mathrm{layer}})}_{t,\tau}(u)\|\leq d_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}\,\mathbf{1}[t=\tau]+\lambda_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}\,K_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}(t,\tau)\,\mathbf{1}[\tau<t].

(74)

Then, for every
x\in\mathcal{X}_{0}
, every
0\leq\tau<t\leq T-1
, and every depth
N_{\mathrm{layer}}\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\|J^{\mathrm{e2e},(N_{\mathrm{layer}})}_{t,\tau}(x)\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\mathrm{layer}}}\ \sum_{1\leq n_{\mathrm{layer},1}<\cdots<n_{\mathrm{layer},k}\leq N_{\mathrm{layer}}}\left(\prod_{m\notin\{n_{\mathrm{layer},1},\dots,n_{\mathrm{layer},k}\}}d_{m}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\sum_{\tau=i_{0}<i_{1}<\cdots<i_{k}=t}\ \prod_{r=1}^{k}\lambda_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}\,K_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}(i_{r},i_{r-1}).

(75)

Moreover, for the diagonal blocks one has

\|J^{\mathrm{e2e},(N_{\mathrm{layer}})}_{t,t}(x)\|\leq\prod_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}=1}^{N_{\mathrm{layer}}}d_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}.

Proof.

For each layer
n_{\mathrm{layer}}
, define the scalar diagonal kernel

\mathcal{D}_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}(t,\tau):=d_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}\,\mathbf{1}[t=\tau],

and the scalar strictly lower-triangular kernel

\mathcal{G}_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}(t,\tau):=\lambda_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}K_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}(t,\tau)\,\mathbf{1}[\tau<t].

Then (
74
) says precisely that

\|J^{(n_{\mathrm{layer}})}_{t,\tau}(u)\|\leq\mathcal{D}_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}(t,\tau)+\mathcal{G}_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}(t,\tau)\qquad\forall u\in\mathcal{X}_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}-1}.

We prove by induction on the depth
p\in\{1,\dots,N_{\mathrm{layer}}\}
that

\left\|\frac{\partial h_{t}^{(p)}(x)}{\partial h_{\tau}^{(0)}(x)}\right\|\leq\bigl[(\mathcal{D}_{p}+\mathcal{G}_{p})\cdots(\mathcal{D}_{1}+\mathcal{G}_{1})\bigr](t,\tau)\qquad(0\leq\tau\leq t\leq T-1).

(76)

For
p=1
, (
76
) is exactly
(
74
) evaluated at
u=x\in\mathcal{X}_{0}
.

Assume now that (
76
) holds for some
p-1\geq 1
.
By the chain rule,

\frac{\partial h_{t}^{(p)}(x)}{\partial h_{\tau}^{(0)}(x)}=\sum_{j=\tau}^{t}\frac{\partial F_{p,t}(h^{(p-1)}(x))}{\partial h_{j}^{(p-1)}(x)}\cdot\frac{\partial h_{j}^{(p-1)}(x)}{\partial h_{\tau}^{(0)}(x)}.

Taking operator norms and using submultiplicativity gives

\left\|\frac{\partial h_{t}^{(p)}(x)}{\partial h_{\tau}^{(0)}(x)}\right\|\leq\sum_{j=\tau}^{t}\left\|\frac{\partial F_{p,t}(h^{(p-1)}(x))}{\partial h_{j}^{(p-1)}(x)}\right\|\cdot\left\|\frac{\partial h_{j}^{(p-1)}(x)}{\partial h_{\tau}^{(0)}(x)}\right\|.

Since
h^{(p-1)}(x)\in\mathcal{X}_{p-1}
, the one-block bound
(
74
) applies:

\left\|\frac{\partial F_{p,t}(h^{(p-1)}(x))}{\partial h_{j}^{(p-1)}(x)}\right\|\leq\mathcal{D}_{p}(t,j)+\mathcal{G}_{p}(t,j).

Using the induction hypothesis for the second factor, we get

\left\|\frac{\partial h_{t}^{(p)}(x)}{\partial h_{\tau}^{(0)}(x)}\right\|\leq\sum_{j=\tau}^{t}(\mathcal{D}_{p}+\mathcal{G}_{p})(t,j)\,\bigl[(\mathcal{D}_{p-1}+\mathcal{G}_{p-1})\cdots(\mathcal{D}_{1}+\mathcal{G}_{1})\bigr](j,\tau).

This is exactly

\bigl[(\mathcal{D}_{p}+\mathcal{G}_{p})\cdots(\mathcal{D}_{1}+\mathcal{G}_{1})\bigr](t,\tau),

which proves (
76
) for depth
p
.

Taking
p=N_{\mathrm{layer}}
yields

\|J^{\mathrm{e2e},(N_{\mathrm{layer}})}_{t,\tau}(x)\|\leq\bigl[(\mathcal{D}_{N_{\mathrm{layer}}}+\mathcal{G}_{N_{\mathrm{layer}}})\cdots(\mathcal{D}_{1}+\mathcal{G}_{1})\bigr](t,\tau).

We now expand the right-hand side.
Since each
\mathcal{D}_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}
is diagonal and equals
d_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}I
as a kernel,
one has the exact product expansion

(\mathcal{D}_{N_{\mathrm{layer}}}+\mathcal{G}_{N_{\mathrm{layer}}})\cdots(\mathcal{D}_{1}+\mathcal{G}_{1})=\sum_{S\subseteq\{1,\dots,N_{\mathrm{layer}}\}}\left(\prod_{m\notin S}d_{m}\right)\prod_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}\in S}^{\rightarrow}\mathcal{G}_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}},

where the ordered product is taken in increasing layer order.
For
\tau<t
, the empty-set term vanishes because it is purely diagonal.
Thus

\|J^{\mathrm{e2e},(N_{\mathrm{layer}})}_{t,\tau}(x)\|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\mathrm{layer}}}\ \sum_{1\leq n_{\mathrm{layer},1}<\cdots<n_{\mathrm{layer},k}\leq N_{\mathrm{layer}}}\left(\prod_{m\notin\{n_{\mathrm{layer},1},\dots,n_{\mathrm{layer},k}\}}d_{m}\right)(\mathcal{G}_{n_{\mathrm{layer},k}}\cdots\mathcal{G}_{n_{\mathrm{layer},1}})(t,\tau).

Finally, by repeated expansion of the kernel product,

(\mathcal{G}_{n_{\mathrm{layer},k}}\cdots\mathcal{G}_{n_{\mathrm{layer},1}})(t,\tau)=\sum_{\tau=i_{0}<i_{1}<\cdots<i_{k}=t}\ \prod_{r=1}^{k}\lambda_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}\,K_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}(i_{r},i_{r-1}),

which gives (
75
).

For the diagonal blocks
\tau=t
, only the empty-set term survives, hence

\|J^{\mathrm{e2e},(N_{\mathrm{layer}})}_{t,t}(x)\|\leq\prod_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}=1}^{N_{\mathrm{layer}}}d_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}.

∎

H.3
A harmonic-kernel bound

For diffuse Transformer blocks the one-block kernel depends on the query time
t
.
The next lemma gives the corresponding convolution bound for

\mathcal{H}(t,\tau):=\frac{1}{t+1}\mathbf{1}[\tau<t].

Lemma H.1

(Nested harmonic bound)
.

Fix
k\geq 1
and define

\mathcal{H}(t,\tau):=\frac{1}{t+1}\mathbf{1}[\tau<t].

Then for every
0\leq\tau<t\leq T-1
,

(\mathcal{H}^{k})(t,\tau)\leq\frac{1}{t+1}\cdot\frac{H_{t}^{\,k-1}}{(k-1)!},

(77)

where

H_{t}:=\sum_{m=1}^{t}\frac{1}{m}

is the
t
-th harmonic number, with the convention
H_{0}:=0
.
Consequently, for every fixed
k
,

(\mathcal{H}^{k})(t,\tau)\lesssim_{k}\frac{(\log(1+t))^{k-1}}{t+1}.

Proof.

For
k=1
the claim is immediate:

\mathcal{H}(t,\tau)=\frac{1}{t+1}\mathbf{1}[\tau<t]\leq\frac{1}{t+1}.

Assume now
k\geq 2
.
By the kernel-product expansion,

(\mathcal{H}^{k})(t,\tau)=\sum_{\tau=i_{0}<i_{1}<\cdots<i_{k}=t}\ \prod_{r=1}^{k}\frac{1}{i_{r}+1}.

Since
i_{k}=t
, the last factor is exactly
\frac{1}{t+1}
, hence

(\mathcal{H}^{k})(t,\tau)=\frac{1}{t+1}\sum_{\tau<i_{1}<\cdots<i_{k-1}<t}\ \prod_{r=1}^{k-1}\frac{1}{i_{r}+1}.

Dropping the lower bound
\tau
only enlarges the sum, so

(\mathcal{H}^{k})(t,\tau)\leq\frac{1}{t+1}\sum_{0<i_{1}<\cdots<i_{k-1}<t}\ \prod_{r=1}^{k-1}\frac{1}{i_{r}+1}.

Now expand

\left(\sum_{m=1}^{t-1}\frac{1}{m+1}\right)^{k-1}.

Every strictly increasing
(k-1)
-tuple

0<i_{1}<\cdots<i_{k-1}<t

appears exactly
(k-1)!
times among the ordered monomials in this expansion.
Therefore

\sum_{0<i_{1}<\cdots<i_{k-1}<t}\ \prod_{r=1}^{k-1}\frac{1}{i_{r}+1}\leq\frac{1}{(k-1)!}\left(\sum_{m=1}^{t-1}\frac{1}{m+1}\right)^{k-1}\leq\frac{H_{t}^{\,k-1}}{(k-1)!}.

Substituting this into the previous display gives

(\mathcal{H}^{k})(t,\tau)\leq\frac{1}{t+1}\cdot\frac{H_{t}^{\,k-1}}{(k-1)!},

which is (
77
).

Since
H_{t}\lesssim\log(1+t)
, the logarithmic form follows.
∎

H.4
Model-specific bounds

Proposition 32

(Deep Transformer bound)
.

Assume the hypotheses of Theorem
31
.
Assume in addition that for each layer
n_{\mathrm{layer}}
there exists
a_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}>0
such that

K_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}(t,\tau)\leq\frac{a_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}}{t+1},\qquad\tau<t.

Fix a bounded source family
0\leq\tau\leq\tau_{\max}
.
Then for every
x\in\mathcal{X}_{0}
and every
\ell\geq 1
with
\tau+\ell\leq T-1
,

\left\|J^{\mathrm{e2e},(N_{\mathrm{layer}})}_{\tau+\ell,\tau}(x)\right\|\lesssim_{\tau_{\max},N_{\mathrm{layer}}}\frac{(\log(1+\ell))^{N_{\mathrm{layer}}-1}}{1+\ell}.

Proof.

Fix an ordered layer subset

1\leq n_{\mathrm{layer},1}<\cdots<n_{\mathrm{layer},k}\leq N_{\mathrm{layer}}.

Define

\mathcal{H}(t,\tau):=\frac{1}{t+1}\mathbf{1}[\tau<t].

By the assumption on
K_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}
,

K_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}(i_{r},i_{r-1})\leq a_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}\mathcal{H}(i_{r},i_{r-1})\qquad\forall r.

Therefore

\sum_{\tau=i_{0}<\cdots<i_{k}=t}\ \prod_{r=1}^{k}\lambda_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}K_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}(i_{r},i_{r-1})\leq\left(\prod_{r=1}^{k}\lambda_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}a_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}\right)(\mathcal{H}^{k})(t,\tau).

By Lemma
H.1
,

(\mathcal{H}^{k})(t,\tau)\lesssim_{k}\frac{(\log(1+t))^{k-1}}{t+1}.

Insert this estimate into Theorem
31
:

\|J^{\mathrm{e2e},(N_{\mathrm{layer}})}_{t,\tau}(x)\|\lesssim_{N_{\mathrm{layer}}}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\mathrm{layer}}}\ \sum_{1\leq n_{\mathrm{layer},1}<\cdots<n_{\mathrm{layer},k}\leq N_{\mathrm{layer}}}\left(\prod_{m\notin\{n_{\mathrm{layer},1},\dots,n_{\mathrm{layer},k}\}}d_{m}\right)\left(\prod_{r=1}^{k}\lambda_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}a_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}\right)\frac{(\log(1+t))^{k-1}}{t+1}.

Since
N_{\mathrm{layer}}
is fixed, the finite sum is bounded by

C_{N_{\mathrm{layer}}}\frac{(\log(1+t))^{N_{\mathrm{layer}}-1}}{t+1}.

Now restrict to the bounded source family
0\leq\tau\leq\tau_{\max}
and set
t=\tau+\ell
.
Then

t+1=\tau+\ell+1\asymp_{\tau_{\max}}1+\ell,\qquad\log(1+t)\asymp_{\tau_{\max}}\log(1+\ell),

uniformly for
0\leq\tau\leq\tau_{\max}
.
Hence

\left\|J^{\mathrm{e2e},(N_{\mathrm{layer}})}_{\tau+\ell,\tau}(x)\right\|\lesssim_{\tau_{\max},N_{\mathrm{layer}}}\frac{(\log(1+\ell))^{N_{\mathrm{layer}}-1}}{1+\ell}.

∎

Proposition 33

(Deep Mamba bound under failed freeze time)
.

Assume the hypotheses of Theorem
31
.
Assume in addition that for each layer
n_{\mathrm{layer}}
there exist
a_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}>0
and
c_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}>0
such that

K_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}(t,\tau)\leq a_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}e^{-c_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}(t-\tau)},\qquad\tau<t.

Set

c_{\ast}:=\min_{1\leq n_{\mathrm{layer}}\leq N_{\mathrm{layer}}}c_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}.

Then for every
x\in\mathcal{X}_{0}
and every
\tau<t
,

\left\|J^{\mathrm{e2e},(N_{\mathrm{layer}})}_{t,\tau}(x)\right\|\lesssim_{N_{\mathrm{layer}}}(1+t-\tau)^{N_{\mathrm{layer}}-1}e^{-c_{\ast}(t-\tau)}.

Proof.

Fix an ordered layer subset

1\leq n_{\mathrm{layer},1}<\cdots<n_{\mathrm{layer},k}\leq N_{\mathrm{layer}}

and write
\ell:=t-\tau
.
For every temporal path
\tau=i_{0}<\cdots<i_{k}=t
, one has

\prod_{r=1}^{k}K_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}(i_{r},i_{r-1})\leq\left(\prod_{r=1}^{k}a_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}\right)\exp\left(-\sum_{r=1}^{k}c_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}(i_{r}-i_{r-1})\right)\leq\left(\prod_{r=1}^{k}a_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}\right)e^{-c_{\ast}\ell}.

The number of strictly increasing temporal paths

\tau=i_{0}<i_{1}<\cdots<i_{k}=t

is the number of compositions of
\ell
into
k
positive integers, namely

\binom{\ell-1}{k-1},

with the convention that this is
0
if
\ell<k
.
Therefore

\sum_{\tau=i_{0}<\cdots<i_{k}=t}\ \prod_{r=1}^{k}\lambda_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}K_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}(i_{r},i_{r-1})\leq\left(\prod_{r=1}^{k}\lambda_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}a_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}\right)\binom{\ell-1}{k-1}e^{-c_{\ast}\ell}.

Insert this estimate into Theorem
31
:

\|J^{\mathrm{e2e},(N_{\mathrm{layer}})}_{t,\tau}(x)\|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\mathrm{layer}}}\ \sum_{1\leq n_{\mathrm{layer},1}<\cdots<n_{\mathrm{layer},k}\leq N_{\mathrm{layer}}}\left(\prod_{m\notin\{n_{\mathrm{layer},1},\dots,n_{\mathrm{layer},k}\}}d_{m}\right)\left(\prod_{r=1}^{k}\lambda_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}a_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}\right)\binom{\ell-1}{k-1}e^{-c_{\ast}\ell}.

Since
N_{\mathrm{layer}}
is fixed and

\binom{\ell-1}{k-1}\lesssim_{k}(1+\ell)^{k-1},

the finite sum is bounded by a constant multiple of

(1+\ell)^{N_{\mathrm{layer}}-1}e^{-c_{\ast}\ell}.

∎

Proposition 34

(Deep Sessa bound)
.

Assume the hypotheses of Theorem
31
.
Assume in addition that for each layer
n_{\mathrm{layer}}
there exist
a_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}>0
and a common exponent

\beta_{\mathrm{tail}}\in(0,1)
such that

K_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}(t,\tau)\leq a_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}(t-\tau)^{-\beta_{\mathrm{tail}}}\bigl(1+\log(1+t-\tau)\bigr),\qquad\tau<t.

Then for every
x\in\mathcal{X}_{0}
and every
\tau<t
,

\left\|J^{\mathrm{e2e},(N_{\mathrm{layer}})}_{t,\tau}(x)\right\|\lesssim_{N_{\mathrm{layer}},\beta_{\mathrm{tail}}}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\mathrm{layer}}}(t-\tau)^{k(1-\beta_{\mathrm{tail}})-1}\bigl(1+\log(1+t-\tau)\bigr)^{k}.

In particular, since
N_{\mathrm{layer}}
is fixed,

\left\|J^{\mathrm{e2e},(N_{\mathrm{layer}})}_{t,\tau}(x)\right\|\lesssim_{N_{\mathrm{layer}},\beta_{\mathrm{tail}}}(t-\tau)^{N_{\mathrm{layer}}(1-\beta_{\mathrm{tail}})-1}\bigl(1+\log(1+t-\tau)\bigr)^{N_{\mathrm{layer}}}.

Proof.

Fix
\tau<t
and write
\ell:=t-\tau
.
Fix an ordered layer subset

1\leq n_{\mathrm{layer},1}<\cdots<n_{\mathrm{layer},k}\leq N_{\mathrm{layer}}.

For every temporal path
\tau=i_{0}<\cdots<i_{k}=t
, set

m_{r}:=i_{r}-i_{r-1}\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}.

Then

m_{1}+\cdots+m_{k}=\ell.

Using the bound on
K_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}
,

\prod_{r=1}^{k}K_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}(i_{r},i_{r-1})\leq\left(\prod_{r=1}^{k}a_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}\right)\prod_{r=1}^{k}m_{r}^{-\beta_{\mathrm{tail}}}\bigl(1+\log(1+m_{r})\bigr).

Since every
m_{r}\leq\ell
, one has

1+\log(1+m_{r})\leq 1+\log(1+\ell).

Therefore

\prod_{r=1}^{k}K_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}(i_{r},i_{r-1})\leq\left(\prod_{r=1}^{k}a_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}\right)\bigl(1+\log(1+\ell)\bigr)^{k}\prod_{r=1}^{k}m_{r}^{-\beta_{\mathrm{tail}}}.

Summing over all temporal paths from
\tau
to
t
gives

\displaystyle\sum_{\tau=i_{0}<\cdots<i_{k}=t}\ \prod_{r=1}^{k}\lambda_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}K_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}(i_{r},i_{r-1})

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left(\prod_{r=1}^{k}\lambda_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}a_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}\right)\bigl(1+\log(1+\ell)\bigr)^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1},\dots,m_{k}\geq 1\\
m_{1}+\cdots+m_{k}=\ell\end{subarray}}m_{1}^{-\beta_{\mathrm{tail}}}\cdots m_{k}^{-\beta_{\mathrm{tail}}}.

The remaining sum is exactly the
k
-fold positive-lag convolution

f_{\beta_{\mathrm{tail}}}^{(*k)}(\ell),\qquad f_{\beta_{\mathrm{tail}}}(n):=n^{-\beta_{\mathrm{tail}}}.

By Theorem
30
,

f_{\beta_{\mathrm{tail}}}^{(*k)}(\ell)\lesssim_{k,\beta_{\mathrm{tail}}}\ell^{k(1-\beta_{\mathrm{tail}})-1}.

Hence

\sum_{\tau=i_{0}<\cdots<i_{k}=t}\ \prod_{r=1}^{k}\lambda_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}K_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}(i_{r},i_{r-1})\lesssim_{k,\beta_{\mathrm{tail}}}\left(\prod_{r=1}^{k}\lambda_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}a_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}\right)\ell^{k(1-\beta_{\mathrm{tail}})-1}\bigl(1+\log(1+\ell)\bigr)^{k}.

Insert this estimate into Theorem
31
and sum over

k=1,\dots,N_{\mathrm{layer}}.

Since
N_{\mathrm{layer}}
is fixed, the finite sum yields the stated bound.

The final simplified estimate follows because, for
\beta_{\mathrm{tail}}\in(0,1)
, the exponent

k(1-\beta_{\mathrm{tail}})-1

is increasing in
k
, so the
k=N_{\mathrm{layer}}
term dominates the smaller-
k
terms up to a constant.
∎

H.5
Horizon-uniform bounds

We now state the horizon-uniform version used in
Section
4.2.7
.

Theorem 35

(Horizon-uniform residual calculus)
.

Fix a depth
N_{\mathrm{layer}}\geq 1
.
For each horizon
T\geq 1
, let

h^{(0,T)}=x\in\mathcal{X}_{0}^{(T)},\qquad h^{(n_{\mathrm{layer}},T)}=F_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}^{(T)}\bigl(h^{(n_{\mathrm{layer}}-1,T)}\bigr),\qquad n_{\mathrm{layer}}=1,\dots,N_{\mathrm{layer}},

where
\mathcal{X}_{0}^{(T)}\subset(\mathbb{R}^{D})^{T}
is compact and each

F_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}^{(T)}
is causal and continuously differentiable on the relevant compact set

\mathcal{X}_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}-1}^{(T)}:=F_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}-1}^{(T)}\circ\cdots\circ F_{1}^{(T)}(\mathcal{X}_{0}^{(T)}).

Define the full end-to-end Jacobian blocks by

J^{\mathrm{e2e},(N_{\mathrm{layer}})}_{t,\tau}(x;T):=\frac{\partial h_{t}^{(N_{\mathrm{layer}},T)}(x)}{\partial h_{\tau}^{(0,T)}(x)}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D},\qquad 0\leq\tau\leq t\leq T-1.

Assume that for each layer
n_{\mathrm{layer}}
there exist constants

d_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}\geq 0,\qquad\lambda_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}\geq 0,

independent of
T
, and a scalar lower-triangular kernel

K_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}:\{(t,\tau):0\leq\tau<t<\infty\}\to[0,\infty)

independent of
T
, such that for every horizon
T\geq 1
,
every
u\in\mathcal{X}_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}-1}^{(T)}
, and every

0\leq\tau\leq t\leq T-1
,

\left\|\frac{\partial F_{n_{\mathrm{layer}},t}^{(T)}(u)}{\partial u_{\tau}}\right\|\leq d_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}\,\mathbf{1}[t=\tau]+\lambda_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}\,K_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}(t,\tau)\,\mathbf{1}[\tau<t].

Then for every horizon
T\geq 1
, every
x\in\mathcal{X}_{0}^{(T)}
, and every

0\leq\tau<t\leq T-1
,

\displaystyle\left\|J^{\mathrm{e2e},(N_{\mathrm{layer}})}_{t,\tau}(x;T)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\mathrm{layer}}}\ \sum_{1\leq n_{\mathrm{layer},1}<\cdots<n_{\mathrm{layer},k}\leq N_{\mathrm{layer}}}\left(\prod_{m\notin\{n_{\mathrm{layer},1},\dots,n_{\mathrm{layer},k}\}}d_{m}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\sum_{\tau=i_{0}<i_{1}<\cdots<i_{k}=t}\ \prod_{r=1}^{k}\lambda_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}\,K_{n_{\mathrm{layer},r}}(i_{r},i_{r-1}).

(78)

Moreover,

\left\|J^{\mathrm{e2e},(N_{\mathrm{layer}})}_{t,t}(x;T)\right\|\leq\prod_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}=1}^{N_{\mathrm{layer}}}d_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}.

In particular, the right-hand side of
(
78
) is independent of
T
.

Proof.

Fix a horizon
T\geq 1
.
Apply Theorem
31
to the horizon-
T
stack

F_{1}^{(T)},\dots,F_{N_{\mathrm{layer}}}^{(T)}

on the compact input set
\mathcal{X}_{0}^{(T)}
.
The hypotheses of Theorem
31
are satisfied with the same
layerwise constants
d_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}},\lambda_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}
and the same kernels
K_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}
,
because these are assumed to be independent of
T
.
Therefore, for this fixed horizon
T
, Theorem
31

gives exactly the path-sum bound
(
78
) and the same diagonal estimate.

Since the displayed right-hand side contains no dependence on
T
, the same bound
holds verbatim for every horizon
T\geq 1
.
∎

Corollary H.2

(Horizon-uniform decay bounds)
.

Assume the hypotheses of Theorem
35
.

(i)

Transformer.

Assume that for each layer
n_{\mathrm{layer}}
there exists
a_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}>0
such that

K_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}(t,\tau)\leq\frac{a_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}}{t+1},\qquad\tau<t.

Fix a bounded source family
0\leq\tau\leq\tau_{\max}
.
Then

\sup_{T\geq\tau_{\max}+\ell+1}\ \sup_{0\leq\tau\leq\tau_{\max}}\ \sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{0}^{(T)}}\left\|J^{\mathrm{e2e},(N_{\mathrm{layer}})}_{\tau+\ell,\tau}(x;T)\right\|\lesssim_{\tau_{\max},N_{\mathrm{layer}}}\frac{(\log(1+\ell))^{N_{\mathrm{layer}}-1}}{1+\ell}.

(ii)

Mamba.

Assume that for each layer
n_{\mathrm{layer}}
there exist
a_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}>0
and
c_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}>0
such that

K_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}(t,\tau)\leq a_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}e^{-c_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}(t-\tau)},\qquad\tau<t.

Set
c_{\ast}:=\min_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}c_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}
.
Then

\sup_{T\geq\ell+1}\ \sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T-\ell-1}\ \sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{0}^{(T)}}\left\|J^{\mathrm{e2e},(N_{\mathrm{layer}})}_{\tau+\ell,\tau}(x;T)\right\|\lesssim_{N_{\mathrm{layer}}}(1+\ell)^{N_{\mathrm{layer}}-1}e^{-c_{\ast}\ell}.

(iii)

Sessa.

Assume that for each layer
n_{\mathrm{layer}}
there exist
a_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}>0
and a common exponent

\beta_{\mathrm{tail}}\in(0,1)
such that

K_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}(t,\tau)\leq a_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}(t-\tau)^{-\beta_{\mathrm{tail}}}\bigl(1+\log(1+t-\tau)\bigr),\qquad\tau<t.

Then

\sup_{T\geq\ell+1}\ \sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T-\ell-1}\ \sup_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{0}^{(T)}}\left\|J^{\mathrm{e2e},(N_{\mathrm{layer}})}_{\tau+\ell,\tau}(x;T)\right\|\lesssim_{N_{\mathrm{layer}},\beta_{\mathrm{tail}}}\sum_{k=1}^{N_{\mathrm{layer}}}\ell^{k(1-\beta_{\mathrm{tail}})-1}\bigl(1+\log(1+\ell)\bigr)^{k}.

In particular, if
N_{\mathrm{layer}}(1-\beta_{\mathrm{tail}})<1
, then the right-hand side tends to
0
as
\ell\to\infty
.

Proof.

Apply Theorem
35
and then repeat exactly the
kernel-class estimates used in the proofs of
Propositions
32
,

33
, and
34
.
Because the layerwise envelope parameters are horizon-uniform, the resulting constants
are independent of
T
.
Taking the indicated suprema over all admissible horizons therefore leaves the bounds unchanged.
For the Transformer case, the passage from
t=\tau+\ell
to
1+\ell
is uniform on bounded-source
families
0\leq\tau\leq\tau_{\max}
.
For the Sessa case, the final asymptotic decay to
0
occurs exactly when the largest power

\ell^{N_{\mathrm{layer}}(1-\beta_{\mathrm{tail}})-1}

has negative exponent, i.e. when
N_{\mathrm{layer}}(1-\beta_{\mathrm{tail}})<1
.
∎

Appendix I
Universal approximation for Sessa with adapters

I.1
Preliminaries and notation

Fix
T\geq 3
and
d_{\mathrm{ext}}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
.
Inputs are

x=(x_{0},\dots,x_{T-1})\in(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}})^{T}\cong\mathbb{R}^{T\times d_{\mathrm{ext}}},

and outputs are in
\mathbb{R}^{T\times d_{\mathrm{ext}}}
.
For
X\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times d_{\mathrm{ext}}}
define

\|X\|_{F}^{2}=\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\|X_{t}\|_{2}^{2}.

Let
\mathcal{D}\subset\mathbb{R}^{T\times d_{\mathrm{ext}}}
be compact and

M_{\mathcal{D}}:=\sup_{x\in\mathcal{D}}\|x\|_{F}<\infty.

Hence
\|x_{t}\|_{2}\leq M_{\mathcal{D}}
for all
x\in\mathcal{D}
and all
t
.

Definition 10

(Causality)
.

F:\mathcal{D}\to\mathbb{R}^{T\times d_{\mathrm{ext}}}
is causal if for every
t
and all
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}
,

x_{0:t}=x^{\prime}_{0:t}
implies
F(x)_{t}=F(x^{\prime})_{t}
.

Lemma I.1

(Prefix factorization of continuous causal maps)
.

Let

\mathcal{D}\subset\mathbb{R}^{T\times d_{\mathrm{ext}}}

be compact and let

F:\mathcal{D}\to\mathbb{R}^{T\times d_{\mathrm{ext}}}

be continuous and causal.
For each
t\in\{0,\dots,T-1\}
, define

p_{t}:\mathcal{D}\to(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}})^{t+1},\qquad p_{t}(x):=x_{0:t},

and

\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{pref}}_{t}:=p_{t}(\mathcal{D}).

Then there exists a unique continuous map

\widehat{F}_{t}:\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{pref}}_{t}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}}

such that

\widehat{F}_{t}(x_{0:t})=F(x)_{t}\qquad\forall x\in\mathcal{D}.

Proof.

Uniqueness is immediate because
p_{t}
is surjective onto
\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{pref}}_{t}
.

Causality ensures that
\widehat{F}_{t}
is well defined: if
p_{t}(x)=p_{t}(x^{\prime})
, then
x_{0:t}=x^{\prime}_{0:t}
, hence

F(x)_{t}=F(x^{\prime})_{t}.

Let

\operatorname{pr}_{t}:\mathbb{R}^{T\times d_{\mathrm{ext}}}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}},\qquad\operatorname{pr}_{t}(y):=y_{t},

and define

g_{t}:=\operatorname{pr}_{t}\circ F:\mathcal{D}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}}.

Then

g_{t}=\widehat{F}_{t}\circ p_{t}.

Let
C\subset\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}}
be closed.
Since
g_{t}
is continuous,
g_{t}^{-1}(C)
is closed in the compact set
\mathcal{D}
, hence compact.
Applying
p_{t}
, the image

p_{t}\big(g_{t}^{-1}(C)\big)

is compact in
\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{pref}}_{t}
, hence closed because
\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{pref}}_{t}
is Hausdorff.
Moreover,

\widehat{F}_{t}^{-1}(C)=p_{t}\big(g_{t}^{-1}(C)\big).

Therefore
\widehat{F}_{t}
is continuous.
∎

I.2
Architecture and function classes

Sessa blocks of width
m

Fix an even query–key width
d_{k}\in 2\mathbb{N}
, a model width
m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,
and a tokenwise pre-normalization map

\Norm:\mathbb{R}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}

applied independently to each token.
We consider two choices:

\Norm=\Id\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Norm=\LN_{\varepsilon_{\ln}}\ \ (\varepsilon_{\ln}>0).

A width-
m
Sessa block is the block of Section
3
specialized to model width
m
,
and we use the following RoPE convention throughout this section.

Write every
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}}
as

z=(z^{(0)},z^{(1)},\dots,z^{(d_{k}/2-1)}),\qquad z^{(r)}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}.

Fix a RoPE base
\vartheta>1
and define the standard pairwise frequencies

\omega_{r}:=\vartheta^{-2r/d_{k}},\qquad r=0,\dots,d_{k}/2-1.

In particular,

\omega_{0}=1.

For every
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
define

\mathrm{RoPE}_{\tau}(z):=\big(R_{\omega_{0}\tau}z^{(0)},R_{\omega_{1}\tau}z^{(1)},\dots,R_{\omega_{d_{k}/2-1}\tau}z^{(d_{k}/2-1)}\big),

where
R_{\theta}
denotes the planar rotation by angle
\theta
.
In the architecture,
\tau=t\in\{0,\dots,T-1\}
; in the constructions below we also allow shifts such as
\tau=-\ell
.
All diagonalization arguments use only the first rotary pair.
Hence, whenever
q,k\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}}
are supported on that first pair,

\langle\mathrm{RoPE}_{t}(q),\mathrm{RoPE}_{j}(k)\rangle=\langle R_{t}q_{1:2},\,R_{j}k_{1:2}\rangle.

The comparison RoPE-Transformer class uses the same convention.

Parameters and dimensions

W^{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times 2m},\qquad b^{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2m},\qquad W^{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times m},\qquad b^{\mathrm{out}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m},

W_{Qf},W_{Kf},W_{Qb},W_{Kb}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times d_{k}},\qquad W_{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times m},

w^{\gamma}\in\mathbb{R}^{m},\qquad b^{\gamma}\in\mathbb{R}.

Tokenwise preprocessing

Given
x\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times m}
:

\displaystyle\tilde{x}_{t}

\displaystyle=\Norm(x_{t})\in\mathbb{R}^{m},

\displaystyle u_{t}

\displaystyle=\tilde{x}_{t}W^{\mathrm{in}}+b^{\mathrm{in}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2m},

\displaystyle u_{t}

\displaystyle=(a_{t},g_{t}),\qquad a_{t},g_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{m},

\displaystyle\bar{a}_{t}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{GELU}(a_{t})\in\mathbb{R}^{m}.

Attention-feedback operator

We fix the attention scale to

\sigma_{k}:=d_{k}^{-1/2}.

Define

q^{f}_{t}=\bar{a}_{t}W_{Qf},\qquad k^{f}_{t}=\bar{a}_{t}W_{Kf},\qquad v_{t}=\bar{a}_{t}W_{V},\qquad q^{b}_{t}=\bar{a}_{t}W_{Qb},\qquad k^{b}_{t}=\bar{a}_{t}W_{Kb},

with

q^{f}_{t},k^{f}_{t},q^{b}_{t},k^{b}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}},\qquad v_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}.

For the causal forward branch
(j\leq t)
, define

\tilde{q}^{f}_{t}=\mathrm{RoPE}_{t}(q^{f}_{t}),\qquad\tilde{k}^{f}_{j}=\mathrm{RoPE}_{j}(k^{f}_{j}),

and define

\alpha^{\mathrm{fwd}}_{t,j}=\frac{\exp\!\Big(\sigma_{k}\langle\tilde{q}^{f}_{t},\tilde{k}^{f}_{j}\rangle\Big)\mathbf{1}[j\leq t]}{\sum_{\tau\leq t}\exp\!\Big(\sigma_{k}\langle\tilde{q}^{f}_{t},\tilde{k}^{f}_{\tau}\rangle\Big)},\qquad f_{t}=\sum_{j\leq t}\alpha^{\mathrm{fwd}}_{t,j}v_{j}.

For the strictly lower feedback branch
(j<t)
, define

\alpha^{\mathrm{fb}}_{t,j}=\frac{\exp\!\Big(\sigma_{k}\langle q^{b}_{t},k^{b}_{j}\rangle\Big)\mathbf{1}[j<t]}{\sum_{\tau<t}\exp\!\Big(\sigma_{k}\langle q^{b}_{t},k^{b}_{\tau}\rangle\Big)},\qquad\alpha^{\mathrm{fb}}_{0,\cdot}=0.

\gamma_{t}=\tanh\!\big(\langle\bar{a}_{t},w^{\gamma}\rangle+b^{\gamma}\big)\in(-1,1).

\big[B_{\mathrm{fb}}\big]_{t,j}=\gamma_{t}\alpha^{\mathrm{fb}}_{t,j},\qquad\big[B_{\mathrm{fb}}\big]_{t,j}=0\ \text{for }j\geq t.

The mixer output is defined by the exact solve

(I-B_{\mathrm{fb}})s=f.

Since
B_{\mathrm{fb}}
is strictly lower triangular, the system has a unique solution.

Residual update

y_{t}=x_{t}+\big((s_{t}\odot g_{t})W^{\mathrm{out}}+b^{\mathrm{out}}\big).

Function classes

Let

\mathrm{ConcreteSessaBlocks}_{\Norm}(d_{k},m)

denote the set of all width-
m
concrete Sessa blocks above with the chosen pre-normalization map
\Norm
.
Define

\Omega_{\mathrm{Sessa},\Norm}^{d_{k}}(m):=\Big\{G_{N_{\mathrm{layer}}}\circ\cdots\circ G_{1}:\ G_{n_{\mathrm{layer}}}\in\mathrm{ConcreteSessaBlocks}_{\Norm}(d_{k},m)\ \text{for all }n_{\mathrm{layer}},\ N_{\mathrm{layer}}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}\Big\}.

Tokenwise input and output adapters

Fix the external data dimension
d_{\mathrm{ext}}
and a model width
m\geq d_{\mathrm{ext}}
.
Define tokenwise affine adapters

\mathrm{Embed}(x)_{t}:=x_{t}W^{\mathrm{emb}}+b^{\mathrm{emb}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m},\qquad\mathrm{Unembed}(h)_{t}:=h_{t}W^{\mathrm{un}}+b^{\mathrm{un}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}}.

Parameters and dimensions

W^{\mathrm{emb}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}\times m},\quad b^{\mathrm{emb}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m},\qquad W^{\mathrm{un}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times d_{\mathrm{ext}}},\quad b^{\mathrm{un}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}}.

\mathrm{Unembed}\circ\mathrm{Embed}=\Id\qquad\text{on }\mathbb{R}^{T\times d_{\mathrm{ext}}}.

We consider Sessa networks of the form

x\ \mapsto\ \mathrm{Unembed}\big(G(\mathrm{Embed}(x))\big),

with

G\in\Omega_{\mathrm{Sessa},\Id}^{d_{k}}(m)

in the main LN-free theorem, and

G\in\Omega_{\mathrm{Sessa},\LN_{\varepsilon_{\ln}}}^{d_{k}}(m)

in the LayerNorm extension.

Causal RoPE-Transformer class

We also define a causal decoder-only RoPE-Transformer class of functions from

\mathbb{R}^{T\times d_{\mathrm{ext}}}\to\mathbb{R}^{T\times d_{\mathrm{ext}}}
,
with internal model width
m
and adapters.

A width-
m
RoPE-Transformer block is a standard decoder block operating on
\mathbb{R}^{T\times m}
:
it consists of causal self-attention with
j\leq t
, RoPE applied to queries and keys in the logits, and fixed scale
\sigma_{k}=d_{k}^{-1/2}
, together with a tokenwise FFN of hidden width
r
and residual connections in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
.
An absolute positional embedding
E\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times m}
is added once at the network input.
Let
\Omega^{H,d_{k},r}_{\mathrm{RoPETr,cau}}(m)
be the set of finite compositions of such blocks on
\mathbb{R}^{T\times m}
.

Finally define the adapted function class

\Omega^{H,d_{k},r}_{\mathrm{RoPETr,cau}}(d_{\mathrm{ext}}\to m\to d_{\mathrm{ext}}):=\Big\{x\mapsto\mathrm{Unembed}\big(\widetilde{g}(\mathrm{Embed}(x)+E)\big):\ \widetilde{g}\in\Omega^{H,d_{k},r}_{\mathrm{RoPETr,cau}}(m),\ E\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times m}\Big\}.

I.3
Softmax lemmas

Lemma I.2

(Softmax concentration)
.

Let
v\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and let
i^{*}=\arg\max_{i}v_{i}
be unique.
Let
\Delta=v_{i^{*}}-\max_{i\neq i^{*}}v_{i}>0
and fix
\delta\in(0,1)
.
For
\sigma_{k}>0
, define
p=\mathrm{softmax}(\sigma_{k}v)
.

p_{i^{*}}\geq 1-\delta\quad\text{whenever}\quad\sigma_{k}\Delta\geq\log\frac{n-1}{\delta}.

Proof.

1-p_{i^{*}}=\frac{\sum_{i\neq i^{*}}e^{\sigma_{k}v_{i}}}{\sum_{i}e^{\sigma_{k}v_{i}}}\leq\frac{(n-1)e^{\sigma_{k}(v_{i^{*}}-\Delta)}}{e^{\sigma_{k}v_{i^{*}}}}=(n-1)e^{-\sigma_{k}\Delta}.

Thus
1-p_{i^{*}}\leq\delta
if
\sigma_{k}\Delta\geq\log\frac{n-1}{\delta}
.
∎

Corollary I.3

(Sharpening at fixed attention scale)
.

Let
v\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and let
i^{*}=\arg\max_{i}v_{i}
be unique.
Let
\Delta=v_{i^{*}}-\max_{i\neq i^{*}}v_{i}>0
, fix
\delta\in(0,1)
, and fix the attention scale

\sigma_{k}>0
.
For
c>0
, define

p^{(c)}:=\mathrm{softmax}(\sigma_{k}\,c^{2}v).

Then

p^{(c)}_{i^{*}}\geq 1-\delta\quad\text{whenever}\quad\sigma_{k}\,c^{2}\Delta\geq\log\frac{n-1}{\delta}.

Thus, in the concrete architecture where
\sigma_{k}=d_{k}^{-1/2}
is fixed,
arbitrarily sharp softmax rows are obtained by scaling the query and key vectors by a common factor
c
.

Proof.

Apply Lemma
I.2
to the logits
c^{2}v
.
∎

Lemma I.4

(Error of an almost one-hot mixture)
.

Let
(w_{j})_{j\in J}\subset\mathbb{R}^{m}
and let
p_{j}\geq 0
,
\sum_{j\in J}p_{j}=1
.
If
p_{j^{*}}\geq 1-\delta
then

\Big\|\sum_{j\in J}p_{j}w_{j}-w_{j^{*}}\Big\|_{2}\leq 2\delta\cdot V_{\max},

where
V_{\max}:=\max_{j\in J}\|w_{j}\|_{2}
.

Proof.

\sum_{j}p_{j}w_{j}-w_{j^{*}}=(p_{j^{*}}-1)w_{j^{*}}+\sum_{j\neq j^{*}}p_{j}w_{j}.

Since
\sum_{j\neq j^{*}}p_{j}=1-p_{j^{*}}\leq\delta
,

\Big\|\sum_{j}p_{j}w_{j}-w_{j^{*}}\Big\|_{2}\leq|1-p_{j^{*}}|\|w_{j^{*}}\|_{2}+\sum_{j\neq j^{*}}p_{j}\|w_{j}\|_{2}\leq 2\delta\,V_{\max},

where
V_{\max}:=\max_{j}\|w_{j}\|_{2}
.
∎

I.4
RoPE diagonalization and triangular solve

Lemma I.5

(RoPE-diagonalization)
.

Fix
T\geq 2
and an even query–key width
d_{k}\in 2\mathbb{N}
.
For any
\delta\in(0,1)
there exists a parameter choice with one head and this
d_{k}

such that for all
t
,

\alpha^{\mathrm{fwd}}_{t,t}\geq 1-\delta,\qquad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\leq t\\
j\neq t\end{subarray}}\alpha^{\mathrm{fwd}}_{t,j}\leq\delta.

At the architectural scale
\sigma_{k}=d_{k}^{-1/2}
, it suffices to scale the
active query/key pair by a common factor
c_{\mathrm{diag}}>0
such that

\sigma_{k}\,c_{\mathrm{diag}}^{2}\Delta_{T}\geq\log\frac{T-1}{\delta},\qquad\Delta_{T}:=1-\max_{s\in\{1,\dots,T-1\}}\cos(s)\ >\ 0.

Proof.

Under the RoPE convention above,
\mathrm{RoPE}_{t}
acts pairwise on consecutive

2
-dimensional coordinates with frequencies
(\omega_{r})_{r=0}^{d_{k}/2-1}
and

\omega_{0}=1.

Activate only the first
2
-dimensional pair by choosing

q_{0}=(1,0,0,\dots,0)\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}},\qquad k_{0}=(1,0,0,\dots,0)\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}},

and then setting

q=c_{\mathrm{diag}}q_{0},\qquad k=c_{\mathrm{diag}}k_{0}.

With RoPE,
\tilde{q}_{t}=\mathrm{RoPE}_{t}(q)
and
\tilde{k}_{j}=\mathrm{RoPE}_{j}(k)
satisfy

\langle\tilde{q}_{t},\tilde{k}_{j}\rangle=c_{\mathrm{diag}}^{2}\cos(t-j),

since all coordinate pairs except the first are identically zero, and the first pair rotates with
frequency
\omega_{0}=1
.
For fixed
t
and
j\leq t
, the unique maximum equals
c_{\mathrm{diag}}^{2}
at
j=t
.
For
j\neq t
,
s=t-j\in\{1,\dots,T-1\}
so
\cos(s)\leq 1-\Delta_{T}
.
Hence the logit gap is at least
c_{\mathrm{diag}}^{2}\Delta_{T}
.
Apply Corollary
I.3
.
∎

Lemma I.6

(Mixing error under diagonalization)
.

Assume
\|v_{j}\|_{2}\leq V_{\max}
. If
\alpha^{\mathrm{fwd}}_{t,t}\geq 1-\delta
, then

\Big\|\sum_{j\leq t}\alpha^{\mathrm{fwd}}_{t,j}v_{j}-v_{t}\Big\|_{2}\leq 2\delta V_{\max},\qquad\|f-v\|_{F}\leq 2\delta V_{\max}\sqrt{T}.

Proof.

Lemma
I.4
with
j^{*}=t
, then sum over
t
.
∎

Lemma I.7

(Lower-triangular inversion)
.

For every input
x
,
B_{\mathrm{fb}}(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times T}
is strictly lower-triangular.
Hence
B_{\mathrm{fb}}(x)
is nilpotent, with
B_{\mathrm{fb}}(x)^{T}=0
.

(I-B_{\mathrm{fb}}(x))^{-1}=\sum_{k=0}^{T-1}B_{\mathrm{fb}}(x)^{k}.

Proof.

A strictly lower-triangular
T\times T
matrix is nilpotent of index at most
T
. Hence
B_{\mathrm{fb}}^{T}=0
, and the Neumann series terminates after
T-1
terms.
∎

I.5
Generating positional codes via feedback

Corollary I.8

(A Sessa block can generate separated positional codes)
.

Fix any tokenwise pre-normalization map

\Norm:\mathbb{R}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}

(applied independently to each token), any even query/key width
d_{k}\geq 2
, and any model width
m\geq 1
.
Then there exists a single width-
m
concrete Sessa block

G^{\mathrm{pos}}\in\mathrm{ConcreteSessaBlocks}_{\Norm}(d_{k},m)

and vectors
p_{0},\dots,p_{T-1}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
such that:

(i)

for all
h\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times m}
and all
t
,

G^{\mathrm{pos}}(h)_{t}=h_{t}+p_{t};

(ii)

for any prescribed unit vector
u\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
, one may choose

p_{t}=(\lambda c_{t})u

with pairwise distinct scalars
(c_{t})_{t=0}^{T-1}
and some
\lambda>0
,
so that on any compact
\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{T\times m}
the scalar sets

\mathcal{I}_{t}:=\{\langle h_{t}+p_{t},u\rangle:\ h\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}\}

are pairwise disjoint after choosing
\lambda
large enough.

Proof.

Fix a prescribed unit vector
u\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
.

The construction does not depend on
\Norm
: setting
W^{\mathrm{in}}=0
gives

u_{t}=\tilde{x}_{t}W^{\mathrm{in}}+b^{\mathrm{in}}=b^{\mathrm{in}},

for all
t
.

Choose
W^{\mathrm{in}}=0
and choose
b^{\mathrm{in}}
so that for every token

a_{t}\equiv a_{\ast}e_{1},\qquad g_{t}\equiv e_{1},

for some
a_{\ast}>0
.
Set

A:=\GELU(a_{\ast})>0.

Then

\bar{a}_{t}=Ae_{1}\qquad\forall t.

Choose

W_{Qf}=0,\qquad W_{Kf}=0.

Then all forward logits vanish, so each forward row is a causal probability vector.
Choose
W_{V}
so that

v_{t}=e_{1}\qquad\forall t.

Therefore

f_{t}=\sum_{j\leq t}\alpha^{\mathrm{fwd}}_{t,j}v_{j}=e_{1}\qquad\forall t.

Choose

W_{Qb}=0,\qquad W_{Kb}=0.

Then for
t\geq 1
,

\alpha^{\mathrm{fb}}_{t,j}=\frac{1}{t}\mathbf{1}[j<t],\qquad\alpha^{\mathrm{fb}}_{0,\cdot}=0.

Fix any constant
\gamma\in(0,1)
, and choose

w^{\gamma}=0,\qquad b^{\gamma}=\operatorname{arctanh}(\gamma).

Then

\gamma_{t}\equiv\gamma,\qquad\big[B_{\mathrm{fb}}\big]_{t,j}=\begin{cases}0,&t=0,\\[2.0pt]
\dfrac{\gamma}{t}\mathbf{1}[j<t],&t\geq 1.\end{cases}

Since
f_{t}=e_{1}
, we have

s_{t}=c_{t}e_{1},

where

c_{0}=1,\qquad c_{t}=1+\frac{\gamma}{t}\sum_{j=0}^{t-1}c_{j}\qquad(t\geq 1).

Let

S_{t}:=\sum_{j=0}^{t}c_{j},\qquad\mu_{t}:=\frac{S_{t}}{t+1}.

Then

S_{t}=\Bigl(1+\frac{\gamma}{t}\Bigr)S_{t-1}+1,

hence

\mu_{t}=\frac{t+\gamma}{t+1}\mu_{t-1}+\frac{1}{t+1},\qquad\mu_{t}-\mu_{t-1}=\frac{1-(1-\gamma)\mu_{t-1}}{t+1}.

Since
\mu_{0}=1<\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
, an induction gives

\mu_{t}<\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\qquad\forall t,

so

\mu_{t}-\mu_{t-1}>0\qquad\forall t\geq 1.

Now

c_{1}=1+\gamma>1=c_{0},

and for
t\geq 1
,

c_{t+1}-c_{t}=\gamma\Bigl(\frac{S_{t}}{t+1}-\frac{S_{t-1}}{t}\Bigr)=\gamma(\mu_{t}-\mu_{t-1})>0.

Therefore
(c_{t})
is strictly increasing.

Choose
W^{\mathrm{out}}
so that its first row is
\lambda u^{\top}
and all other rows are zero, and set

b^{\mathrm{out}}=0
.
Since

s_{t}\odot g_{t}=(c_{t}e_{1})\odot e_{1}=c_{t}e_{1},

the residual update equals

(s_{t}\odot g_{t})W^{\mathrm{out}}=c_{t}(\lambda u)=:p_{t}.

Hence

G^{\mathrm{pos}}(h)_{t}=h_{t}+p_{t}.

Let
\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{T\times m}
be compact and set

R:=\sup_{h\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}\max_{t}\|h_{t}\|_{2}<\infty.

Then

|\langle h_{t},u\rangle|\leq R\qquad\forall h\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}},\ \forall t.

Since the
c_{t}
are pairwise distinct, let

\Delta_{c}:=\min_{s\neq t}|c_{s}-c_{t}|>0.

Choose

\lambda>\frac{2R}{\Delta_{c}}.

Then the shifted scalar sets

\mathcal{I}_{t}=\{\langle h_{t}+p_{t},u\rangle:\ h\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}\}=\{\langle h_{t},u\rangle+\lambda c_{t}:\ h\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}\}

are pairwise disjoint.
∎

I.6
Composition error control

Lemma I.9

(Composition error on thickened compacts)
.

Let
(X,d)
be a metric space such that closed neighborhoods of compact sets are compact,
for example,
X=\mathbb{R}^{n}
with the Euclidean metric.
Fix a compact
\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}_{1}\subset X
and continuous maps
f_{i}:X\to X
for
i=1,\dots,L
.

Fix
\rho_{\mathrm{nbhd}}>0
and define recursively

\widetilde{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}_{1}:=\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}_{1},\qquad\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}_{i+1}:=f_{i}(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}_{i}),\qquad\widetilde{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}_{i+1}:=\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{\rho_{\mathrm{nbhd}}}(\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}_{i+1})=\{x\in X:\ d(x,\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}_{i+1})\leq\rho_{\mathrm{nbhd}}\}.

Then each
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}_{i}
is compact.

For every
\varepsilon>0
there exist tolerances
\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{L}>0
such that:
for any continuous maps
g_{i}:\widetilde{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}_{i}\to X
satisfying, for each
i
,

\sup_{x\in\widetilde{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}_{i}}d\big(f_{i}(x),g_{i}(x)\big)\leq\delta_{i}\quad\text{and}\quad\delta_{i}\leq\rho_{\mathrm{nbhd}},

the compositions
F:=f_{L}\circ\cdots\circ f_{1}
and
G:=g_{L}\circ\cdots\circ g_{1}
are well-defined on
\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}_{1}

(and in fact
g_{i}(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}_{i})\subset\widetilde{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}_{i+1}
), and

\sup_{x\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}_{1}}d\big(F(x),G(x)\big)\leq\varepsilon.

Proof.

Well-definedness is immediate.
Fix
i
and
x\in\widetilde{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}_{i}
. By definition,
f_{i}(x)\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}_{i+1}=f_{i}(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}_{i})
, hence

d\big(f_{i}(x),\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}_{i+1}\big)=0
. Therefore

d\big(g_{i}(x),\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}_{i+1}\big)\leq d\big(g_{i}(x),f_{i}(x)\big)+d\big(f_{i}(x),\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}_{i+1}\big)\leq\delta_{i}\leq\rho_{\mathrm{nbhd}},

so
g_{i}(x)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}_{i+1}
. Thus
g_{i}(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}_{i})\subset\widetilde{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}_{i+1}
and all compositions are defined.

The remainder of the proof is by induction on
L
.
For
L=1
it is immediate.

Assume the claim holds for
L-1
. Let

F_{<L}:=f_{L-1}\circ\cdots\circ f_{1},\qquad G_{<L}:=g_{L-1}\circ\cdots\circ g_{1}.

Since
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}_{L}
is compact and
f_{L}
is continuous,
f_{L}
is uniformly continuous on
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}_{L}
.
Pick
\eta>0
such that

d(u,v)\leq\eta\ \Rightarrow\ d\big(f_{L}(u),f_{L}(v)\big)\leq\varepsilon/2\qquad\forall u,v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}_{L}.

Set
\delta_{L}:=\min(\rho_{\mathrm{nbhd}},\varepsilon/2)
.
By the inductive hypothesis applied with target accuracy
\eta
, choose
\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{L-1}>0
so that

\sup_{x\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}_{1}}d\big(F_{<L}(x),G_{<L}(x)\big)\leq\eta.

Then for
x\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}_{1}
, noting that
G_{<L}(x)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}_{L}
by well-definedness,

d\big(F(x),G(x)\big)\leq d\big(f_{L}(F_{<L}(x)),f_{L}(G_{<L}(x))\big)+d\big(f_{L}(G_{<L}(x)),g_{L}(G_{<L}(x))\big)\leq\varepsilon/2+\delta_{L}\leq\varepsilon.

∎

Lemma I.10

(Tokenwise GELU approximation)
.

Let
S\subset\mathbb{R}^{m}
be compact and let
\Theta:S\to\mathbb{R}^{p}
be continuous.
Then for every
\eta>0
there exist
r\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and affine maps

A:\mathbb{R}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}^{r},\qquad B:\mathbb{R}^{r}\to\mathbb{R}^{p}

such that

\sup_{z\in S}\|B(\mathrm{GELU}(A(z)))-\Theta(z)\|_{2}\leq\eta.

Moreover, if a larger width
r^{\prime}\geq r
is prescribed in advance, the same conclusion still holds with
r^{\prime}
in place of
r
,
by padding the hidden layer with unused coordinates.

Proof.

Apply the standard one-hidden-layer universal approximation theorem for non-polynomial activations coordinatewise to the components of
\Theta
, and concatenate the resulting hidden units into a single hidden layer. Since
\mathrm{GELU}
is continuous and non-polynomial, the theorem applies; see, e.g.,
28
;
34
. The padding claim is immediate by adding hidden coordinates with zero incoming and outgoing weights.
∎

Lemma I.11

(Tokenwise GELU approximation with zero-padding)
.

Let
S\subset\mathbb{R}^{m}
be compact, let
\Theta:S\to\mathbb{R}^{p_{0}}
be continuous,
let
\eta>0
, and let
r_{0}\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
.
For each
r\geq r_{0}
, let

E_{r}:\mathbb{R}^{p_{0}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{p(r)}

be a coordinate zero-padding embedding, where
p(r)
may depend on
r
.
Then there exist
r\geq r_{0}
and affine maps

A:\mathbb{R}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}^{r},\qquad B:\mathbb{R}^{r}\to\mathbb{R}^{p(r)}

such that

\sup_{z\in S}\big\|B(\mathrm{GELU}(A(z)))-E_{r}(\Theta(z))\big\|_{2}\leq\eta.

Proof.

By Lemma
I.10
, there exist

s\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
and affine maps

\bar{A}:\mathbb{R}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}^{s},\qquad\bar{B}:\mathbb{R}^{s}\to\mathbb{R}^{p_{0}}

such that

\sup_{z\in S}\big\|\bar{B}(\mathrm{GELU}(\bar{A}(z)))-\Theta(z)\big\|_{2}\leq\eta.

Set

r:=\max\{r_{0},s\}.

Let

I_{s\to r}:\mathbb{R}^{s}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{r}

be the coordinate zero-padding inclusion into the first
s
coordinates, and let

\Pi_{r\to s}:\mathbb{R}^{r}\to\mathbb{R}^{s}

be the projection onto those first
s
coordinates.
Define

A:=I_{s\to r}\circ\bar{A},\qquad B:=E_{r}\circ\bar{B}\circ\Pi_{r\to s}.

Then
A
is affine and
B
is affine.
Since
\mathrm{GELU}(0)=0
and
\mathrm{GELU}
acts coordinatewise,

\Pi_{r\to s}\big(\mathrm{GELU}(A(z))\big)=\Pi_{r\to s}\big(\mathrm{GELU}(I_{s\to r}\bar{A}(z))\big)=\mathrm{GELU}(\bar{A}(z)).

Hence

B(\mathrm{GELU}(A(z)))=E_{r}\!\big(\bar{B}(\mathrm{GELU}(\bar{A}(z)))\big).

Because
E_{r}
is coordinate zero-padding, it is an isometric embedding for the Euclidean norm, so

\big\|B(\mathrm{GELU}(A(z)))-E_{r}(\Theta(z))\big\|_{2}=\big\|\bar{B}(\mathrm{GELU}(\bar{A}(z)))-\Theta(z)\big\|_{2}.

Taking the supremum over
z\in S
gives the claim.
∎

I.7
Stability of finite-horizon RoPE attention

For fixed
T
, causal RoPE attention depends continuously on the query, key, and value arrays.
The next two lemmas collect the continuity and near-diagonal transport estimates used below.

Lemma I.12

(Stability of finite-horizon RoPE attention)
.

Fix a horizon
T\geq 1
, number of heads
H\geq 1
, even key/query width
d_{k}\geq 2
, value width
d_{v}\geq 1
,
attention scale
\sigma_{k}>0
, and an output matrix

W^{O}\in\mathbb{R}^{Hd_{v}\times m}.

Let
\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{T\times m}
be compact, and define the compact token set

S_{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}:=\{u_{t}:\ u\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}},\ 0\leq t\leq T-1\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{m}.

For each head
a=1,\dots,H
, let

q^{a},k^{a},\widehat{q}^{a},\widehat{k}^{a}:S_{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}},\qquad v^{a},\widehat{v}^{a}:S_{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{v}}

be continuous.
Let
A,\widehat{A}:\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}\to\mathbb{R}^{T\times m}
be the corresponding causal RoPE-attention maps:
for
u\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}
,

A(u)_{t}=\Big(\operatorname{concat}_{a=1}^{H}z_{t}^{a}(u)\Big)W^{O},\qquad z_{t}^{a}(u):=\sum_{j\leq t}\alpha_{t,j}^{a}(u)\,v^{a}(u_{j}),

where

\alpha_{t,j}^{a}(u)=\frac{\exp\!\Big(\sigma_{k}\big\langle\mathrm{RoPE}_{t}(q^{a}(u_{t})),\,\mathrm{RoPE}_{j}(k^{a}(u_{j}))\big\rangle\Big)\mathbf{1}[j\leq t]}{\sum_{\tau\leq t}\exp\!\Big(\sigma_{k}\big\langle\mathrm{RoPE}_{t}(q^{a}(u_{t})),\,\mathrm{RoPE}_{\tau}(k^{a}(u_{\tau}))\big\rangle\Big)},

and similarly
\widehat{A}
is defined from
(\widehat{q}^{a},\widehat{k}^{a},\widehat{v}^{a})
.

Then for every
\varepsilon>0
there exists
\eta>0
such that

\sup_{z\in S_{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}}\max_{1\leq a\leq H}\Big(\|q^{a}(z)-\widehat{q}^{a}(z)\|_{2}+\|k^{a}(z)-\widehat{k}^{a}(z)\|_{2}+\|v^{a}(z)-\widehat{v}^{a}(z)\|_{2}\Big)\leq\eta

implies

\sup_{u\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}\|A(u)-\widehat{A}(u)\|_{F}\leq\varepsilon.

Proof.

Define the finite-dimensional array space

\mathcal{X}:=\Big((\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}})^{H}\Big)^{T}\times\Big((\mathbb{R}^{d_{k}})^{H}\Big)^{T}\times\Big((\mathbb{R}^{d_{v}})^{H}\Big)^{T},

and equip it with the max norm

\|(Q,K,V)\|_{\max}:=\max\Big\{\max_{t,a}\|q_{t}^{a}\|_{2},\ \max_{t,a}\|k_{t}^{a}\|_{2},\ \max_{t,a}\|v_{t}^{a}\|_{2}\Big\}.

Let

\mathcal{A}:\mathcal{X}\to\mathbb{R}^{T\times m}

denote the finite-horizon causal RoPE-attention operator defined by the displayed formulas above.
RoPE attention is continuous as a composition of continuous finite-dimensional operations.

Now define continuous maps

\Xi,\widehat{\Xi}:\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}\to\mathcal{X}

by collecting the tokenwise arrays:

\Xi(u):=\big((q^{a}(u_{t}))_{t,a},\ (k^{a}(u_{t}))_{t,a},\ (v^{a}(u_{t}))_{t,a}\big),

\widehat{\Xi}(u):=\big((\widehat{q}^{a}(u_{t}))_{t,a},\ (\widehat{k}^{a}(u_{t}))_{t,a},\ (\widehat{v}^{a}(u_{t}))_{t,a}\big).

Then

A=\mathcal{A}\circ\Xi,\qquad\widehat{A}=\mathcal{A}\circ\widehat{\Xi}.

The image
\Xi(\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}})\subset\mathcal{X}
is compact. Fix
\eta_{0}>0
; then its closed
\eta_{0}
-neighborhood

\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{\eta_{0}}(\Xi(\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}))

is compact as well. Hence
\mathcal{A}
is uniformly continuous on this neighborhood.
Therefore, for the given
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
\delta>0
such that

x,x^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{\eta_{0}}(\Xi(\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}})),\qquad\|x-x^{\prime}\|_{\max}\leq\delta\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\|\mathcal{A}(x)-\mathcal{A}(x^{\prime})\|_{F}\leq\varepsilon.

Set
\eta:=\min\{\eta_{0},\delta\}
.
If the stated tokenwise bound holds, then for every
u\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}
,

\|\Xi(u)-\widehat{\Xi}(u)\|_{\max}\leq\eta,

because each of the three summands is individually bounded by
\eta
.
In particular,

\widehat{\Xi}(u)\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{\eta_{0}}(\Xi(\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}})).

Applying the uniform continuity estimate to
\Xi(u)
and
\widehat{\Xi}(u)
gives

\|A(u)-\widehat{A}(u)\|_{F}=\|\mathcal{A}(\Xi(u))-\mathcal{A}(\widehat{\Xi}(u))\|_{F}\leq\varepsilon\qquad\forall u\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}.

Taking the supremum over
u\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}
proves the claim.
∎

Lemma I.13

(Near-diagonal attention transports values)
.

Fix a horizon
T\geq 1
, an output width
s\geq 1
, and a compact set

\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{R}^{T\times m}.

Let

S_{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}^{\prime}}:=\{u_{t}:\ u\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}^{\prime},\ 0\leq t\leq T-1\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{m}.

Let
\phi,v:S_{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}^{\prime}}\to\mathbb{R}^{s}
be continuous, and define

M_{\phi}:=\sup_{z\in S_{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}^{\prime}}}\|\phi(z)\|_{2}<\infty.

Suppose a one-head causal attention mechanism on
\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}^{\prime}
produces weights
\alpha_{t,j}(u)
and outputs

f_{t}(u):=\sum_{j\leq t}\alpha_{t,j}(u)\,v(u_{j}),\qquad u\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}^{\prime}.

Assume that for some
\delta\in(0,1)
and
\eta\geq 0
,

\alpha_{t,t}(u)\geq 1-\delta\qquad\forall\,u\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}^{\prime},\ \forall\,t\in\{0,\dots,T-1\},

and

\sup_{z\in S_{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}^{\prime}}}\|v(z)-\phi(z)\|_{2}\leq\eta.

Then

\sup_{u\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}^{\prime}}\max_{0\leq t\leq T-1}\|f_{t}(u)-\phi(u_{t})\|_{2}\leq 2\delta(M_{\phi}+\eta)+\eta.

Proof.

Fix
u\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}^{\prime}
and
t\in\{0,\dots,T-1\}
.
Set

w_{j}:=v(u_{j})\in\mathbb{R}^{s},\qquad 0\leq j\leq t.

Then
(\alpha_{t,j}(u))_{j\leq t}
is a convex distribution and

f_{t}(u)=\sum_{j\leq t}\alpha_{t,j}(u)\,w_{j}.

Moreover,

\|w_{j}\|_{2}\leq\|\phi(u_{j})\|_{2}+\|v(u_{j})-\phi(u_{j})\|_{2}\leq M_{\phi}+\eta\qquad\forall j\leq t.

Since
\alpha_{t,t}(u)\geq 1-\delta
, Lemma
I.4
yields

\|f_{t}(u)-w_{t}\|_{2}\leq 2\delta(M_{\phi}+\eta).

Also,

\|w_{t}-\phi(u_{t})\|_{2}\leq\eta.

Hence

\|f_{t}(u)-\phi(u_{t})\|_{2}\leq\|f_{t}(u)-w_{t}\|_{2}+\|w_{t}-\phi(u_{t})\|_{2}\leq 2\delta(M_{\phi}+\eta)+\eta.

Since this bound is uniform in
u
and
t
, the claim follows.
∎

I.8
Universal approximation for causal RoPE-Transformers with adapters

Lemma I.14

(Universality of causal RoPE-Transformers with adapters)
.

Let

\mathcal{D}\subset\mathbb{R}^{T\times d_{\mathrm{ext}}}

be compact and let

F:\mathcal{D}\to\mathbb{R}^{T\times d_{\mathrm{ext}}}

be continuous and causal.
Then for any
\varepsilon>0
there exist finite
(H,d_{k},r,m)
and

g\in\Omega^{H,d_{k},r}_{\mathrm{RoPETr,cau}}(d_{\mathrm{ext}}\to m\to d_{\mathrm{ext}})

such that

\sup_{x\in\mathcal{D}}\|F(x)-g(x)\|_{F}<\varepsilon.

Moreover, the construction in the proof allows an arbitrary choice of distinct scalars
(c_{t})_{t=0}^{T-1}

in Paragraph 3, hence an arbitrary absolute embedding
E
supported on the pos-scalar coordinate of slice
h=1

with distinct entries.

Proof.

Fix
\varepsilon>0
.

0. Causal factorization

For each
t\in\{0,\dots,T-1\}
, define the compact set of attainable prefixes

\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{pref}}_{t}:=\{(x_{0},\dots,x_{t}):x\in\mathcal{D}\}\subset(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}})^{t+1}.

By Lemma
I.1
, there exists a unique continuous map

\widehat{F}_{t}:\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{pref}}_{t}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}},\qquad\widehat{F}_{t}(x_{0},\dots,x_{t}):=F(x)_{t}\quad(x\in\mathcal{D}).

Since
\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{pref}}_{t}
is compact in Euclidean space, it is closed in
(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}})^{t+1}
.
By Tietze extension applied coordinatewise
(
42
)
, extend
\widehat{F}_{t}
to a continuous map

F_{t}:(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}})^{t+1}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}}

such that
F(x)_{t}=F_{t}(x_{0},\dots,x_{t})
for all
x\in\mathcal{D}
.
Let
M_{\mathcal{D}}:=\sup_{x\in\mathcal{D}}\|x\|_{F}
.

1. Model width

Set the number of heads to be

H:=T+1,\qquad d_{k}:=2,

and choose the per-head value width

d_{v}:=d_{\mathrm{ext}}+2.

Define

m:=H\,d_{v}=(T+1)(d_{\mathrm{ext}}+2).

We index coordinates of
\mathbb{R}^{m}
by head-slices:

\mathbb{R}^{m}\cong\bigoplus_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{R}^{d_{v}},

and within each slice
\mathbb{R}^{d_{v}}
we separate content coordinates, the first
d_{\mathrm{ext}}
coordinates,
a constant coordinate with index
d_{\mathrm{ext}}+1
, and a pos-scalar coordinate with index
d_{\mathrm{ext}}+2
.

2. Adapters

We now fix concrete adapters
\mathrm{Embed},\mathrm{Unembed}
of the form introduced in Paragraph
I.2
.
This choice satisfies
\mathrm{Unembed}\circ\mathrm{Embed}=\mathrm{Id}
on
\mathbb{R}^{T\times d_{\mathrm{ext}}}
.
Define the sequence-level affine adapter

\mathrm{Embed}:\mathbb{R}^{T\times d_{\mathrm{ext}}}\to\mathbb{R}^{T\times m}

tokenwise by placing
x_{t}
into the content coordinates of slice
h=1
,
setting the constant coordinate to
1
, and all other coordinates to
0
:

\mathrm{Embed}(x)_{t}=\Big((x_{t},\ 1,\ 0)\ ;\ 0\ ;\ 0\ ;\ \cdots\ ;\ 0\Big)\in\bigoplus_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}+2}.

This is an affine map
x_{t}\mapsto x_{t}W^{\mathrm{emb}}+b^{\mathrm{emb}}
for suitable
W^{\mathrm{emb}}
and
b^{\mathrm{emb}}
.

Define
\mathrm{Unembed}:\mathbb{R}^{T\times m}\to\mathbb{R}^{T\times d_{\mathrm{ext}}}
tokenwise by reading out the content coordinates of slice
h=1
:

\mathrm{Unembed}(h)_{t}:=\big(h_{t}^{(h=1)}\big)_{1:d_{\mathrm{ext}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}},

which is exactly a coordinate projection (equivalently, an affine map with
b^{\mathrm{un}}=0
) and satisfies

\mathrm{Unembed}\circ\mathrm{Embed}=\mathrm{Id}
on
\mathbb{R}^{T\times d_{\mathrm{ext}}}
.
Thus
\mathrm{Unembed}
is linear and non-expansive in Frobenius norm:

\|\mathrm{Unembed}(U)-\mathrm{Unembed}(U^{\prime})\|_{F}\leq\|U-U^{\prime}\|_{F}\qquad\forall U,U^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times m}.

Let
\bar{x}:=\mathrm{Embed}(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times m}
. The set
\bar{\mathcal{D}}:=\mathrm{Embed}(\mathcal{D})
is compact.

3. Absolute positional code

Choose distinct scalars
c_{0},\dots,c_{T-1}\in\mathbb{R}
and define
E\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times m}
by:

E_{t}\ \text{is zero in all coordinates except the pos-scalar coordinate of slice }h=1,\ \text{where it equals }c_{t}.

Thus for all
x\in\mathcal{D}
and all
t
,

(\bar{x}_{t}+E_{t})^{(h=1)}_{d_{\mathrm{ext}}+2}=c_{t},

i.e. the pos-scalar is exactly
c_{t}
, independent of
x
.

4. Prefix encoding

Fix a diagonalization tolerance
\delta\in(0,1)
, to be chosen sufficiently small later.
Under the standing RoPE convention fixed above, when
d_{k}=2
there is only one rotary pair and

\omega_{0}=1
, so

\mathrm{RoPE}_{t}(z)=R_{t}z

with
R_{t}
the planar rotation by angle
t
radians
(
40
)
.
Construct a single causal RoPE-attention sublayer whose output at time
t
stores

x_{t},\ x_{t-1},\ \dots,\ x_{0}

in the content coordinates of slices
h=2,3,\dots,t+2
, respectively. Equivalently, lag
\ell=0,\dots,t
is stored in slice
h=\ell+2
, and all active slices
h=2,\dots,H
are controlled uniformly via the one-hot estimates below.

Because slice
h=1
has a constant coordinate equal to
1
, we may choose the linear maps
W^{Q}_{h},W^{K}_{h}

so that for every token representation
u
:

q_{t}^{(h)}=(u_{t}^{(h=1)})_{d_{\mathrm{ext}}+1}\,\bar{q}^{(h)}=\bar{q}^{(h)}\in\mathbb{R}^{2},\qquad k_{j}^{(h)}=(u_{j}^{(h=1)})_{d_{\mathrm{ext}}+1}\,\bar{k}=\bar{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{2},

for fixed vectors
\bar{q}^{(h)},\bar{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
Fix a scaling factor
c_{\mathrm{pack}}>0
.
We set
\bar{k}=c_{\mathrm{pack}}(1,0)
and for head
h\in\{2,\dots,H\}
set

\bar{q}^{(h)}:=c_{\mathrm{pack}}\mathrm{RoPE}_{-(h-2)}(1,0)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}.

Under RoPE inside logits, for
j\leq t
,

\left\langle\mathrm{RoPE}_{t}(\bar{q}^{(h)}),\ \mathrm{RoPE}_{j}(\bar{k})\right\rangle=c_{\mathrm{pack}}^{2}\cos\big((t-(h-2))-j\big).

Define for each
(t,h)
the maximizer

j^{*}(t,h)\in\arg\max_{0\leq j\leq t}\cos\big((t-(h-2))-j\big).

For
h\leq t+2
, the unique maximizer is
j^{*}(t,h)=t-(h-2)
, since the maximum value
1
is attained only at argument
0
.
For
h>t+2
, all arguments
(t-(h-2))-j
are distinct negative integers, and the corresponding cosine values are pairwise distinct
(since
\cos(a)=\cos(b)
implies
a=\pm b+2\pi k
for some
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, and for integers
a,b
this forces
k=0
because
2\pi
is irrational, hence
a=\pm b
).
Thus the maximizer is unique for every
(t,h)
.

Let

v_{t,h}(j):=\cos\big((t-(h-2))-j\big),\qquad j\in\{0,\dots,t\},

and for
t\geq 1
define

\Delta_{t,h}:=v_{t,h}\big(j^{*}(t,h)\big)-\max_{j\in\{0,\dots,t\}\setminus\{j^{*}(t,h)\}}v_{t,h}(j)>0.

Since the set of pairs
(t,h)
is finite, the uniform gap

\Delta_{*}:=\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}t\in\{1,\dots,T-1\}\\
h\in\{2,\dots,H\}\end{subarray}}\Delta_{t,h}

is strictly positive.
For
t=0
, the row is exactly one-hot.

Choose
c_{\mathrm{pack}}
such that

\sigma_{k}c_{\mathrm{pack}}^{2}\Delta_{*}\geq\log\frac{T-1}{\delta}.

Then by Corollary
I.3
, for every
x\in\mathcal{D}
, every
t\geq 1
, and every head
h\in\{2,\dots,H\}
,

\alpha^{\mathrm{fwd},(h)}_{t,\,j^{*}(t,h)}\geq 1-\delta.

For
t=0
the distribution is exactly one-hot on
j=0
.

For heads
h=2,\dots,H
, choose
W^{V}_{h}
so that the value vector copies the content coordinates of slice
h=1

(and has zeros in the last two coordinates of the head output):

v_{j}^{(h)}=\big(x_{j},\ 0,\ 0\big)\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}+2}.

For head
h=1
, set
W^{V}_{1}\equiv 0
, so head
1
contributes
0
.

Let
f_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
denote the concatenation of head outputs.
Choose
W^{O}=I_{m}
.
Since slices
h\geq 2
are initially zero, the residual update

h_{t}\leftarrow h_{t}+f_{t}

injects the head outputs directly into these slices.

Let
V_{\max}:=\sup_{x\in\mathcal{D}}\max_{j}\|x_{j}\|_{2}\leq M_{\mathcal{D}}
.
For each
t
and each head
h\in\{2,\dots,H\}
, by Lemma
I.4
,

\left\|\big(f_{t}^{(h)}\big)_{1:d_{\mathrm{ext}}}-x_{j^{*}(t,h)}\right\|_{2}\leq 2\delta\,V_{\max}\leq 2\delta\,M_{\mathcal{D}}.

In particular, for
h\leq t+2
we have
j^{*}(t,h)=t-(h-2)
, hence slices
h=2,\dots,t+2
recover

(x_{t},x_{t-1},\dots,x_{0})
with per-slice content error at most
2\delta\,V_{\max}
.

5. Ideal encoded state and target map

Fix
H:=T+1
heads indexed by
h=1,\dots,H
, with head
h=1
unused as before.
For each
(t,h)
with
t\in\{0,\dots,T-1\}
and
h\in\{2,\dots,H\}
define the deterministic index

j^{*}(t,h)\in\arg\max_{0\leq j\leq t}\ \cos\big((t-(h-2))-j\big).

With the same
c_{\mathrm{pack}}
chosen in Paragraph 4 so that

\sigma_{k}\,c_{\mathrm{pack}}^{2}\Delta_{*}\ \geq\ \log\frac{T-1}{\delta},

Corollary
I.3
gives, for every
x\in\mathcal{D}
, every
t\geq 1
, and every head
h\in\{2,\dots,H\}
,
the causal attention distribution over
j\leq t
satisfies

\alpha^{\mathrm{fwd},(h)}_{t,\,j^{*}(t,h)}\ \geq\ 1-\delta.

For
t=0
the attention is exactly one-hot.

Define
\widehat{h}_{t}(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
, where
m=(T+1)(d_{\mathrm{ext}}+2)
, by
letting slice
h=1
equal
(x_{t},1,c_{t})
in coordinates
(1{:}d_{\mathrm{ext}},\,d_{\mathrm{ext}}{+}1,\,d_{\mathrm{ext}}{+}2)
and zero elsewhere, and for each slice
h=2,\dots,H
placing
x_{j^{*}(t,h)}
in the first
d_{\mathrm{ext}}
coordinates and zeros in the last two;
and set

\widehat{S}:=\{\widehat{h}_{t}(x):x\in\mathcal{D},\ t\in\{0,\dots,T-1\}\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{m}.

Then
\widehat{S}
is compact as a continuous image of a compact set.

For each fixed
t\in\{0,\dots,T-1\}
, define the affine map, in fact linear,

\mathrm{Read}_{t}:\mathbb{R}^{m}\to(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}})^{t+1}

by reading the content coordinates of slices
h=2,\dots,t+2
in reverse order:

\mathrm{Read}_{t}(u):=\Big(\big(u^{(t+2)}\big)_{1:d_{\mathrm{ext}}},\,\big(u^{(t+1)}\big)_{1:d_{\mathrm{ext}}},\,\dots,\,\big(u^{(2)}\big)_{1:d_{\mathrm{ext}}}\Big).

Equivalently, for
\ell=0,\dots,t
,

\big(\mathrm{Read}_{t}(u)\big)_{\ell}=\big(u^{(t-\ell+2)}\big)_{1:d_{\mathrm{ext}}}.

By construction of the ideal encoded state and because
j^{*}(t,h)=t-(h-2)
for
h\leq t+2
,

\mathrm{Read}_{t}\big(\widehat{h}_{t}(x)\big)=(x_{0},\dots,x_{t})\qquad\forall x\in\mathcal{D}.

Thus the pos-scalar coordinate identifies
t
, while the encoded slices determine the prefix
(x_{0},\dots,x_{t})
.

Decompose
\widehat{S}
as the finite disjoint union
\widehat{S}=\bigsqcup_{t=0}^{T-1}\widehat{S}_{t}
where

\widehat{S}_{t}:=\{\widehat{h}_{t}(x):x\in\mathcal{D}\}
.
Each
\widehat{S}_{t}
is compact and contained in the affine hyperplane

\{u\in\mathbb{R}^{m}:\ (u^{(h=1)})_{d_{\mathrm{ext}}+2}=c_{t}\}
.
Since the scalars
c_{t}
are distinct, the sets
\widehat{S}_{t}
are pairwise separated. Therefore
\widehat{\Phi}
is continuous on
\widehat{S}
once each restriction
\widehat{\Phi}|_{\widehat{S}_{t}}
is continuous.
Now fix
t
. For every
u=\widehat{h}_{t}(x)\in\widehat{S}_{t}
, by the defining property of
F_{t}
from Paragraph 0 and by the
readout identity above,

\widehat{\Phi}(u)=F(x)_{t}=F_{t}(x_{0},\dots,x_{t})=F_{t}\!\big(\mathrm{Read}_{t}(u)\big).

Therefore

\widehat{\Phi}|_{\widehat{S}_{t}}=F_{t}\circ\mathrm{Read}_{t}|_{\widehat{S}_{t}}.

\mathrm{Read}_{t}
is a linear map, and
F_{t}:(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}})^{t+1}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}}
is continuous, so

\widehat{\Phi}|_{\widehat{S}_{t}}
is continuous.
Thus
\widehat{\Phi}
is continuous on
\widehat{S}
.

By Tietze extension applied coordinatewise, extend
\widehat{\Phi}
to a continuous
\widetilde{\Phi}:\mathbb{R}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}}
.

6. FFN approximation

Let
h^{\mathrm{enc}}_{t}(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
denote the token state after the first RoPE-attention block,
constructed in Paragraph 4, with
W^{O}=I_{m}
, head
h=1
set to zero, and the FFN set to zero.
Slice
h=1
is unchanged by the residual, since the concatenated head output has zero slice
h=1
,
so
(h^{\mathrm{enc}}_{t}(x))^{(h=1)}=(x_{t},1,c_{t})
exactly.

For each head slice
h\in\{2,\dots,H\}
, by the encoding construction in Paragraph 4 we have

\|v_{j}^{(h)}\|_{2}\leq V_{\max}
and
\alpha^{\mathrm{fwd},(h)}_{t,\,j^{*}(t,h)}\geq 1-\delta
.
Therefore Lemma
I.4
gives, for each
x\in\mathcal{D}
, each
t
, each
h\in\{2,\dots,H\}
,

\left\|\big(h^{\mathrm{enc}}_{t}(x)\big)^{(h)}_{1:d_{\mathrm{ext}}}-x_{j^{*}(t,h)}\right\|_{2}\ \leq\ 2\delta V_{\max},

and the last two coordinates of each slice are exactly zero on both sides.
Therefore, for each
(x,t)
,

\big\|h^{\mathrm{enc}}_{t}(x)-\widehat{h}_{t}(x)\big\|_{2}\ \leq\ \sqrt{\sum_{h=2}^{H}(2\delta V_{\max})^{2}}\ =\ 2\delta V_{\max}\sqrt{T}.

In particular,

\sup_{x\in\mathcal{D}}\max_{t}\ \big\|h^{\mathrm{enc}}_{t}(x)-\widehat{h}_{t}(x)\big\|_{2}\ \leq\ 2\delta V_{\max}\sqrt{T}.

Let

S_{\mathrm{enc}}:=\{h^{\mathrm{enc}}_{t}(x):x\in\mathcal{D},\ t=0,\dots,T-1\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{m}

(compact).
Since
\widehat{S}
is compact, for every radius
r_{\mathrm{nbhd}}>0
the closed neighborhood

\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{r_{\mathrm{nbhd}}}(\widehat{S}):=\{u\in\mathbb{R}^{m}:\ \mathrm{dist}(u,\widehat{S})\leq r_{\mathrm{nbhd}}\}

is compact. Fix such an
r_{\mathrm{nbhd}}>0
.

By uniform continuity of
\widetilde{\Phi}
on the compact set
\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{r_{\mathrm{nbhd}}}(\widehat{S})
, there exists a continuity tolerance

\delta_{\mathrm{UC}}>0

such that

u,v\in\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{r_{\mathrm{nbhd}}}(\widehat{S}),\qquad\|u-v\|_{2}\leq\delta_{\mathrm{UC}}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\|\widetilde{\Phi}(u)-\widetilde{\Phi}(v)\|_{2}\leq\varepsilon/(3\sqrt{T}).

Now choose the diagonalization parameter
\delta\in(0,1)
above small enough so that

2\delta V_{\max}\sqrt{T}\leq\min\{r_{\mathrm{nbhd}},\delta_{\mathrm{UC}}\}.

Then
S_{\mathrm{enc}}\subset\overline{\mathcal{N}}_{r_{\mathrm{nbhd}}}(\widehat{S})
, and for all
x\in\mathcal{D}
and all
t
,

\|h^{\mathrm{enc}}_{t}(x)-\widehat{h}_{t}(x)\|_{2}\leq\delta_{\mathrm{UC}}.

Hence

\|\widetilde{\Phi}(h^{\mathrm{enc}}_{t}(x))-\widetilde{\Phi}(\widehat{h}_{t}(x))\|_{2}\leq\varepsilon/(3\sqrt{T}).

Since
\widetilde{\Phi}(\widehat{h}_{t}(x))=\widehat{\Phi}(\widehat{h}_{t}(x))=F(x)_{t}
by construction, it follows that

\|\widetilde{\Phi}(h^{\mathrm{enc}}_{t}(x))-F(x)_{t}\|_{2}\leq\varepsilon/(3\sqrt{T}).

Define the continuous map
\Psi:S_{\mathrm{enc}}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}}
by

\Psi(u):=\widetilde{\Phi}(u)-\big(u^{(h=1)}\big)_{1:d_{\mathrm{ext}}},

i.e. the increment needed (in slice
h=1
content) to turn the current content into
\widetilde{\Phi}(u)
.
By the universal approximation theorem for tokenwise GELU FFNs
(
34
;
28
)
, there exists a tokenwise FFN (hidden width
r
large enough)
whose output
\mathrm{FFN}(h)_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
is supported only on slice
h=1
content coordinates and satisfies

\sup_{u\in S_{\mathrm{enc}}}\left\|\big(\mathrm{FFN}(u)\big)^{(h=1)}_{1:d_{\mathrm{ext}}}-\Psi(u)\right\|_{2}\ \leq\ \varepsilon/(3\sqrt{T}),

and
\mathrm{FFN}(u)
equals
0
on all other coordinates.
Applying this tokenwise, define the sequence-level FFN by
\mathrm{FFN}(h)_{t}:=\mathrm{FFN}(h_{t})
.
Using the residual connection in the second block (with its attention set to zero), the slice
h=1
content becomes

\big(h^{\mathrm{enc}}_{t}(x)\big)^{(h=1)}_{1:d_{\mathrm{ext}}}+\big(\mathrm{FFN}(h^{\mathrm{enc}}_{t}(x))\big)^{(h=1)}_{1:d_{\mathrm{ext}}}\approx\widetilde{\Phi}\big(h^{\mathrm{enc}}_{t}(x)\big)\approx F(x)_{t}.

Combining the encoding and FFN errors yields for each
t

\left\|\big(h^{\mathrm{out}}_{t}(x)\big)^{(h=1)}_{1:d_{\mathrm{ext}}}-F(x)_{t}\right\|_{2}\leq\varepsilon/\sqrt{T},

hence
\|F(x)-g(x)\|_{F}\leq\varepsilon
uniformly on
\mathcal{D}
after applying
\mathrm{Unembed}
.
∎

I.9
Direct Sessa building blocks

Storage decomposition

Fix a model width

m=(T+1)d_{\mathrm{ext}}+2.

Write
\mathbb{R}^{m}
as the orthogonal direct sum of coordinate subspaces

\mathbb{R}^{m}=U_{0}\oplus U_{1}\oplus\cdots\oplus U_{T-1}\oplus U_{\mathrm{out}}\oplus\mathrm{span}\{e_{\mathrm{const}},e_{\mathrm{pos}}\},

where each
U_{\ell}
is a coordinate copy of
\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}}
and

U_{\mathrm{out}}
is a coordinate copy of
\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}}
.

Fix linear isometries

J_{\ell}:\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}}\to U_{\ell}\qquad(\ell=0,\dots,T-1),\qquad J_{\mathrm{out}}:\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}}\to U_{\mathrm{out}},

and let

R_{\ell}:=J_{\ell}^{-1}:U_{\ell}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}},\qquad R_{\mathrm{out}}:=J_{\mathrm{out}}^{-1}:U_{\mathrm{out}}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}}.

Let
\pi_{\ell}:\mathbb{R}^{m}\to U_{\ell}
denote the projection onto
U_{\ell}
, let
\pi_{\mathrm{out}}:\mathbb{R}^{m}\to U_{\mathrm{out}}
denote the projection onto
U_{\mathrm{out}}
, and let

\pi_{\mathrm{st}}:\mathbb{R}^{m}\to U_{0}\oplus\cdots\oplus U_{T-1}\oplus\mathrm{span}\{e_{\mathrm{const}},e_{\mathrm{pos}}\}

denote the projection onto the storage slice.

For each
\ell\in\{1,\dots,T-1\}
, let

T_{0\to\ell}:=J_{\ell}\circ R_{0}:U_{0}\to U_{\ell}

denote the fixed coordinate-copy isomorphism, and let

T_{0\to\mathrm{out}}:=J_{\mathrm{out}}\circ R_{0}:U_{0}\to U_{\mathrm{out}}

denote the corresponding copy map into the output slice.

Let

\iota_{\mathrm{st}}:\pi_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathbb{R}^{m})\to\mathbb{R}^{m}

denote the linear lift obtained by restoring the output slice as the copy of
U_{0}
, i.e.

\pi_{\mathrm{st}}(\iota_{\mathrm{st}}(z))=z,\qquad\pi_{\mathrm{out}}(\iota_{\mathrm{st}}(z))=T_{0\to\mathrm{out}}(\pi_{0}(z)).

Lemma I.15

(Uniform small-signal linearization of GELU)
.

Let
K\subset\mathbb{R}^{q}
be compact.
Then

\sup_{u\in K}\left\|\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\,\mathrm{GELU}(\varepsilon u)-u\right\|_{2}\longrightarrow 0\qquad\text{as }\varepsilon\downarrow 0.

Consequently, for every compact
K\subset\mathbb{R}^{p}
, every linear map
L:\mathbb{R}^{p}\to\mathbb{R}^{q}
, and every
\eta>0
,
there exists
\varepsilon>0
such that

\sup_{z\in K}\left\|\frac{2}{\varepsilon}\,\mathrm{GELU}(\varepsilon Lz)-Lz\right\|_{2}\leq\eta.

Proof.

\mathrm{GELU}
is
C^{1}
and
\mathrm{GELU}^{\prime}(0)=1/2
. Hence

\mathrm{GELU}(u)=\frac{1}{2}u+r(u),\qquad\frac{\|r(u)\|_{2}}{\|u\|_{2}}\to 0\quad\text{as }u\to 0.

Apply this uniformly on the compact set
\varepsilon K
.
The second statement follows by substituting
u=Lz
.
∎

Lemma I.16

(A single Sessa block copies one lag into a dedicated slice)
.

Fix
\ell\in\{1,\dots,T-1\}
and a compact set
\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{T\times m}
.
Define the compact source-token set

S_{0}:=\{\pi_{0}(h_{t}):\ h\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}},\ 0\leq t\leq T-1\}\subset U_{0}.

Then for every
\eta>0
there exists a width-
m
concrete Sessa block

G^{\mathrm{lag}}_{\ell}\in\mathrm{ConcreteSessaBlocks}_{\Id}(2,m)

such that:

(i)

feedback is turned off identically, i.e.
\gamma_{t}\equiv 0
;

(ii)

for every
h\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}
and every
t
, the block can be chosen so that its input projection depends only on the source slice
U_{0}
(and fixed biases), i.e. it ignores all coordinates in
U_{r}
for
r\neq 0
, as well as
U_{\mathrm{out}}
,
e_{\mathrm{const}}
, and
e_{\mathrm{pos}}
;

\pi_{r}(G^{\mathrm{lag}}_{\ell}(h)_{t})=\pi_{r}(h_{t})\qquad\text{for all }r\in\{0,\dots,T-1\}\setminus\{\ell\},

and the coordinates in
U_{\mathrm{out}}
,
e_{\mathrm{const}}
, and
e_{\mathrm{pos}}
are unchanged;

(iii)

if

j^{*}(t,\ell)\in\arg\max_{0\leq j\leq t}\cos\big((t-\ell)-j\big),

then

\sup_{h\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}\max_{0\leq t\leq T-1}\left\|\pi_{\ell}\big(G^{\mathrm{lag}}_{\ell}(h)_{t}\big)-\pi_{\ell}(h_{t})-T_{0\to\ell}\!\big(\pi_{0}(h_{j^{*}(t,\ell)})\big)\right\|_{2}\leq\eta.

In particular, for
t\geq\ell
one has
j^{*}(t,\ell)=t-\ell
.

Proof.

Reserve one coordinate of
a_{t}
for a constant bias so that the corresponding coordinate of
\bar{a}_{t}
is strictly positive.
Fix a diagonalization tolerance
\delta\in(0,1)
, to be chosen sufficiently small later.
Choose
W_{Qf},W_{Kf}
so that only the designated constant coordinate of
\bar{a}_{t}
contributes to the forward queries and keys, and set

q^{f}_{t}\equiv q^{(\ell)}_{\mathrm{diag}}:=c_{\ell}\,\mathrm{RoPE}_{-\ell}(1,0),\qquad k^{f}_{t}\equiv k_{\mathrm{diag}}:=c_{\ell}(1,0)\in\mathbb{R}^{2},

for some scale
c_{\ell}>0
.
Then for
j\leq t
,

\big\langle\mathrm{RoPE}_{t}(q^{f}_{t}),\,\mathrm{RoPE}_{j}(k^{f}_{j})\big\rangle=c_{\ell}^{2}\cos\big((t-\ell)-j\big).

For each
t
, the maximizer of
j\mapsto\cos((t-\ell)-j)
on
\{0,\dots,t\}
is unique; denote it by
j^{*}(t,\ell)
.
Uniqueness is proved as in Lemma
I.5
: for
t\geq\ell
, the maximizer is
j=t-\ell
, while for
t<\ell
the arguments are distinct negative integers and therefore yield distinct cosine values.
Hence, by the proof of Lemma
I.5
together with Corollary
I.3
, after choosing
c_{\ell}
large enough we obtain

\alpha_{t,j^{*}(t,\ell)}\geq 1-\delta\qquad\forall t=0,\dots,T-1.

Use
d_{\mathrm{ext}}
further coordinates of
a_{t}
to encode the source slice via

a^{\mathrm{src}}_{t}=\varepsilon\,\pi_{0}(h_{t})\in U_{0}.

By Lemma
I.15
, after choosing
\varepsilon>0
small enough, these coordinates of

\bar{a}_{t}=\mathrm{GELU}(a_{t})

can be linearly mapped by
W_{V}
to approximate
T_{0\to\ell}(\pi_{0}(h_{t}))
uniformly on the compact source-token set
S_{0}
.
Choose
W_{V}
so that the resulting value vector lives only in the destination slice
U_{\ell}
.
Set
g\equiv\mathbf{1}
, set
W^{\mathrm{out}}
to be the identity on
U_{\ell}
and zero on all other coordinates,
and set
b^{\mathrm{out}}=0
.
Choose the feedback branch identically zero.

Define the compact token set

S_{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}:=\{h_{t}:\ h\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}},\ 0\leq t\leq T-1\}\subset\mathbb{R}^{m},

and let

\phi(z):=T_{0\to\ell}(\pi_{0}(z)),\qquad z\in S_{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}.

Set

M_{\ell}:=\sup_{z\in S_{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}}\|\phi(z)\|_{2}<\infty.

Choose the small-signal approximation so that the induced value map
v:S_{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}\to U_{\ell}
satisfies

\sup_{z\in S_{\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}}}\|v(z)-\phi(z)\|_{2}\leq\eta_{\mathrm{val}}.

Then for every
h\in\mathcal{K}\_{\mathrm{set}}
and every
t
, Lemma
I.4
applied to

f_{t}(h)=\sum_{j\leq t}\alpha_{t,j}\,v(h_{j})

with distinguished index
j^{*}(t,\ell)
gives

\big\|f_{t}(h)-v(h_{j^{*}(t,\ell)})\big\|_{2}\leq 2\delta\,(M_{\ell}+\eta_{\mathrm{val}}).

Therefore

\big\|f_{t}(h)-\phi(h_{j^{*}(t,\ell)})\big\|_{2}\leq 2\delta\,(M_{\ell}+\eta_{\mathrm{val}})+\eta_{\mathrm{val}}.

Since

\phi(h_{j^{*}(t,\ell)})=T_{0\to\ell}\big(\pi_{0}(h_{j^{*}(t,\ell)})\big),

choosing
\delta
and
\eta_{\mathrm{val}}
sufficiently small makes the total error at most
\eta
.
All remaining coordinates are unchanged by construction.
∎

Lemma I.17

(A diagonal Sessa block realizes a block of tokenwise GELU units)
.

Let

A:\pi_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathbb{R}^{m})\to\mathbb{R}^{q}

be affine, with

q\in\{1,\dots,m-1\},

and let

B:\mathbb{R}^{q}\to U_{\mathrm{out}}

be linear.
Fix a compact set

S\subset\pi_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathbb{R}^{m}).

Then for every
\eta>0
there exists a width-
m
concrete Sessa block

G^{\mathrm{batch}}\in\mathrm{ConcreteSessaBlocks}_{\Id}(2,m)

such that:

(i)

feedback is turned off identically;

(ii)

the storage coordinates are preserved exactly:

\pi_{\mathrm{st}}(G^{\mathrm{batch}}(h)_{t})=\pi_{\mathrm{st}}(h_{t})\qquad\forall h,\forall t;

(iii)

the input projection ignores the current output slice, i.e. it depends only on
\pi_{\mathrm{st}}(h_{t})
;

(iv)

for every sequence
h
whose tokenwise storage states lie in
S
,

\sup_{t}\left\|\pi_{\mathrm{out}}(G^{\mathrm{batch}}(h)_{t})-\pi_{\mathrm{out}}(h_{t})-B(\mathrm{GELU}(A(\pi_{\mathrm{st}}(h_{t}))))\right\|_{2}\leq\eta.

Proof.

Let the first
q
coordinates of
a_{t}
encode the affine preactivations

A(\pi_{\mathrm{st}}(h_{t})).

Reserve one additional coordinate of
a_{t}
for a constant bias so that the corresponding coordinate of
\bar{a}_{t}
is strictly positive.
Choose
W_{Qf},W_{Kf}
so that only that coordinate contributes to the forward queries and keys, yielding constant queries and keys that make the forward attention arbitrarily close to diagonal uniformly in
t
by Lemma
I.5
.

Choose
W_{V}
so that the resulting value vector equals

B(\bar{a}_{1:q})\in U_{\mathrm{out}}

in the output slice and is zero on the storage slice.
Choose
g\equiv\mathbf{1}
, choose
W^{\mathrm{out}}
to be the identity on
U_{\mathrm{out}}
and zero on the storage slice,
set
b^{\mathrm{out}}=0
, and set the columns of the input projection corresponding to the current output slice
U_{\mathrm{out}}
to zero.
Choose the feedback branch identically zero.

Let

\phi(z):=B(\mathrm{GELU}(A(z))),\qquad z\in S,

and set

M_{\phi}:=\sup_{z\in S}\|\phi(z)\|_{2}<\infty.

Because the input projection ignores the current output slice, the preactivations
a_{t}
depend only on
\pi_{\mathrm{st}}(h_{t})
, hence for every sequence
h
whose tokenwise storage states lie in
S
, the resulting value vector is exactly

v_{t}=\phi(\pi_{\mathrm{st}}(h_{t}))\in U_{\mathrm{out}}.

By the diagonal forward-attention construction, after choosing the diagonalization tolerance
\delta\in(0,1)
sufficiently small we have

\alpha_{t,t}\geq 1-\delta\qquad\forall t=0,\dots,T-1.

Therefore, for every such sequence
h
and every
t
, Lemma
I.4
applied to

f_{t}=\sum_{j\leq t}\alpha_{t,j}v_{j}

with distinguished index
j^{*}=t
gives

\|f_{t}-v_{t}\|_{2}\leq 2\delta M_{\phi}.

Choosing
\delta
so that
2\delta M_{\phi}\leq\eta
(trivial if
M_{\phi}=0
) yields

\sup_{t}\left\|f_{t}-\phi(\pi_{\mathrm{st}}(h_{t}))\right\|_{2}\leq\eta.

Since the residual update is added only in
U_{\mathrm{out}}
, this gives the desired conclusion.
∎

Corollary I.18

(Tokenwise GELU approximation by stacked Sessa blocks)
.

Let

S\subset\pi_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathbb{R}^{m})

be compact and let

\Theta:S\to U_{\mathrm{out}}

be continuous.
Then for every
\eta>0
there exists a finite composition

G^{\mathrm{tok}}=G_{M}^{\mathrm{batch}}\circ\cdots\circ G_{1}^{\mathrm{batch}},\qquad G_{b}^{\mathrm{batch}}\in\mathrm{ConcreteSessaBlocks}_{\Id}(2,m),

such that:

(i)

every
G_{b}^{\mathrm{batch}}
preserves the storage slice exactly and ignores the current output slice in its input projection;

(ii)

for every sequence
h
whose tokenwise storage states lie in
S
,

\pi_{\mathrm{st}}(G^{\mathrm{tok}}(h)_{t})=\pi_{\mathrm{st}}(h_{t})\qquad\forall t,

and

\sup_{t}\left\|\pi_{\mathrm{out}}(G^{\mathrm{tok}}(h)_{t})-\pi_{\mathrm{out}}(h_{t})-\Theta(\pi_{\mathrm{st}}(h_{t}))\right\|_{2}\leq\eta.

Proof.

By Lemma
I.10
, for every
\eta^{\prime}>0
there exist
a width
R\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
, an affine map

A_{\mathrm{tot}}:\pi_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathbb{R}^{m})\to\mathbb{R}^{R},

and an affine map

B_{\mathrm{tot}}:\mathbb{R}^{R}\to U_{\mathrm{out}}

such that

\sup_{z\in S}\left\|B_{\mathrm{tot}}(\mathrm{GELU}(A_{\mathrm{tot}}(z)))-\Theta(z)\right\|_{2}\leq\eta^{\prime}.

Write

B_{\mathrm{tot}}(u)=L_{\mathrm{tot}}u+b_{\mathrm{tot}},

where

L_{\mathrm{tot}}:\mathbb{R}^{R}\to U_{\mathrm{out}}

is linear and

b_{\mathrm{tot}}\in U_{\mathrm{out}}.

Partition the
R
hidden units into batches of size at most
m-1
:

R=q_{1}+\cdots+q_{M},\qquad 1\leq q_{b}\leq m-1.

Write accordingly

A_{\mathrm{tot}}=(A_{1},\dots,A_{M}),

with each

A_{b}:\pi_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathbb{R}^{m})\to\mathbb{R}^{q_{b}}

affine, and decompose the linear map
L_{\mathrm{tot}}
as

L_{\mathrm{tot}}(u^{(1)},\dots,u^{(M)})=\sum_{b=1}^{M}L_{b}u^{(b)},

where each

L_{b}:\mathbb{R}^{q_{b}}\to U_{\mathrm{out}}

is linear.

Choose
\eta^{\prime}>0
so that

\eta^{\prime}\leq\eta/2

and

\sup_{z\in S}\left\|B_{\mathrm{tot}}(\mathrm{GELU}(A_{\mathrm{tot}}(z)))-\Theta(z)\right\|_{2}\leq\eta^{\prime}.

Apply Lemma
I.17
to each pair
(A_{b},L_{b})
with accuracy

\frac{\eta}{2(M+1)}.

This yields concrete Sessa batch blocks

G^{\mathrm{batch}}_{b}\in\mathrm{ConcreteSessaBlocks}_{\Id}(2,m),\qquad b=1,\dots,M,

such that each block preserves the storage slice exactly, ignores the current output slice in its input projection,
and contributes

L_{b}(\mathrm{GELU}(A_{b}(\cdot)))

to the output slice up to error at most
\eta/(2(M+1))
.

It remains to represent the constant term
b_{\mathrm{tot}}
.
Choose the scalar constant hidden map

A_{\mathrm{const}}:\pi_{\mathrm{st}}(\mathbb{R}^{m})\to\mathbb{R},\qquad A_{\mathrm{const}}(z)\equiv 1,

and the linear map

L_{\mathrm{const}}:\mathbb{R}\to U_{\mathrm{out}},\qquad L_{\mathrm{const}}(\xi):=\frac{\xi}{\mathrm{GELU}(1)}\,b_{\mathrm{tot}}.

Then

L_{\mathrm{const}}(\mathrm{GELU}(A_{\mathrm{const}}(z)))=b_{\mathrm{tot}}\qquad\forall z\in S.

Apply Lemma
I.17
once more to
(A_{\mathrm{const}},L_{\mathrm{const}})
,
again with accuracy

\frac{\eta}{2(M+1)}.

Since each batch block preserves storage exactly and ignores the current output slice in its input projection,
all blocks act on the same storage input and their contributions add in
U_{\mathrm{out}}
.
Hence the cumulative implementation error of the
M
linear batches together with the one constant batch is at most

(M+1)\cdot\frac{\eta}{2(M+1)}=\frac{\eta}{2}.

Combining this with the approximation error
\eta^{\prime}\leq\eta/2
gives the total error bound
\eta
.
∎

I.10
Sessa universality for causal maps

Theorem

(Universal approximation for Sessa with adapters)
.

Let
\mathcal{D}\subset\mathbb{R}^{T\times d_{\mathrm{ext}}}
be compact and let

F:\mathcal{D}\to\mathbb{R}^{T\times d_{\mathrm{ext}}}

be continuous and causal.
Then for any
\varepsilon>0
there exist a model width
m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
,
an even key/query width
d_{k}
(in fact
d_{k}=2
suffices),
tokenwise adapters

\mathrm{Embed}:\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}}\to\mathbb{R}^{m},\qquad\mathrm{Unembed}:\mathbb{R}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}},

and a finite-depth network

G\in\Omega_{\mathrm{Sessa},\Id}^{d_{k}}(m)

consisting only of the concrete Sessa blocks from Section
3
,
such that

\sup_{x\in\mathcal{D}}\Big\|F(x)-\mathrm{Unembed}\big(G(\mathrm{Embed}(x))\big)\Big\|_{F}<\varepsilon.

Proof of Theorem
14
.

Fix
\varepsilon>0
.

Step 0: causal factorization.

For each
t\in\{0,\dots,T-1\}
, define the compact set of attainable prefixes

\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{pref}}_{t}:=\{(x_{0},\dots,x_{t}):x\in\mathcal{D}\}\subset(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}})^{t+1}.

By Lemma
I.1
, there exists a unique continuous map

\widehat{F}_{t}:\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{pref}}_{t}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}},\qquad\widehat{F}_{t}(x_{0},\dots,x_{t}):=F(x)_{t}\quad(x\in\mathcal{D}).

Since
\mathcal{P}^{\mathrm{pref}}_{t}
is compact in Euclidean space, it is closed in
(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}})^{t+1}
.
By Tietze extension applied coordinatewise, extend
\widehat{F}_{t}
to a continuous map

F_{t}:(\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}})^{t+1}\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}}

such that

F(x)_{t}=F_{t}(x_{0},\dots,x_{t})\qquad\forall x\in\mathcal{D}.

Step 1: width and adapters

Set

m:=(T+1)d_{\mathrm{ext}}+2.

Use the storage decomposition introduced above.

Define the tokenwise embedding by

\mathrm{Embed}(x)_{t}=J_{0}(x_{t})+J_{\mathrm{out}}(x_{t})+e_{\mathrm{const}},

that is, place
x_{t}
in both
U_{0}
and
U_{\mathrm{out}}
, set the constant coordinate to
1
, and set all other coordinates to
0
.

Define
\mathrm{Unembed}
tokenwise by

\mathrm{Unembed}(h)_{t}:=R_{\mathrm{out}}(\pi_{\mathrm{out}}(h_{t}))\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathrm{ext}}}.

Then

\mathrm{Unembed}(\mathrm{Embed}(x))=x\qquad\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{T\times d_{\mathrm{ext}}},

\mathrm{Embed}(\mathcal{D})
is compact, and
\mathrm{Unembed}
is linear and non-expansive in Frobenius norm.

Step 2: positional code

Apply Corollary
I.8
with
